One secondary source treated *Ad Vitellionem Paralipomena* and *Astronomiae Pars Optica* as two separate works; they are one book, and the full title carries both. **Quoted verbatim from the primary opinion text:** *Griswold v. Connecticut*, **381 U.S. 479 (1965)**, Douglas, J. — "specific guarantees in the Bill of Rights have penumbras, formed by emanations from those guarantees that help give them life and substance." Confirmed at Cornell LII after Justia returned 403. **Verified:** Astrup, Siesjö & Symon, "Thresholds in cerebral ischemia — the ischemic penumbra," *Stroke* **12(6):723–725 (1981)**, PMID **6272455** — paired measurements of rCBF, evoked potential, pH and extracellular K⁺ in a baboon MCA-occlusion model gave **two** thresholds, electrical failure at ~70% flow reduction and massive K⁺ release at ~90%, with the penumbra the zone between: functionally silent, structurally intact, salvageable on reperfusion. **Zone definitions quoted from NASA Goddard's eclipse glossary** for umbra, penumbra and antumbra; the antumbra definition ("the Sun appears larger than the Moon which is seen in complete silhouette") is what spread 3's argument rests on. **CUT during the check — a too-good-to-check anecdote.** The story that Symon coined the term while walking across Guildford Street to lunch at Queen Square appears in secondary accounts **with no citation anywhere in the peer-reviewed literature**, and does not appear in the most substantial four-decade review of the concept. Charming, unsourced, cut — the exact shape `too-good-to-check.html` exists to catch, and it would have been the zine's best anecdote. **DOWNGRADED rather than shipped:** the claim that Astrup's group *consciously reasoned from eclipse geometry* when naming it. Several reviews assert the astronomical derivation; the four-decade review is **silent on both the derivation and the coiner**, and no primary statement by the authors could be reached. The page therefore says only that the word is Kepler's and medicine took it — **true regardless of intent** — and the colophon records the downgrade. **Also caught before it shipped:** one secondary source described the ischaemic penumbra as named for "the halo surrounding a total solar eclipse." That is the **corona**, not the penumbra; the error is not repeated on the page. **Given as approximate and flagged as such:** umbral track width (~100s of km) and penumbral extent (thousands of km) are stated as an order-of-magnitude ratio with **no single figure quoted for either**, because both vary substantially with a given eclipse's geometry, and spread 4 needs only that the ratio is not close. **Held honestly on the page:** spread 9 states the sustained criticism of the *Griswold* penumbra reasoning **in the body text rather than a footnote**, and the zine takes no position on substantive due process. Spread 11 exists to close off the reading that two fields borrowing a word is *evidence about disability* — it is a fact about the usefulness of a distinction, not a finding — and the "Not:" set refuses the severity-spectrum reading first, since "mild to severe" is the two-state vocabulary wearing a gradient costume. Spread 10 is marked on the page as a rhyme, not a proof. **The fermata pull-quote on spread 5 is Ryan Boren's own**, written during the 8 April 2024 totality over Central Texas, and is credited to him by name and date. **Open (1):** Kepler's *authorship of the coinage* is confirmed only in the sense that the word appears in his 1604 text in the act of being defined — **no search was made for an earlier use by another writer**, so "coined" rests on the convergent testimony of etymological reference works. **Open (2):** the photometric shape on spread 5 (~100-fold fall in the last two minutes, another order of magnitude in the final half minute, >4 orders of magnitude overall) follows published eclipse-illuminance measurements but was **not read at a single named paper**; Figure 3 is therefore labelled *schematic* on its face with an unlabelled axis, and the colophon says it must not be read off as data. **Open (3):** that the corona is "roughly as bright as a full Moon" is standard eclipse-observing physics taken from observing literature rather than a primary photometric source. **Extended 2026-08-15 — spread 11 added, because the piece was conceding the thing it exists to refuse.** Ryan noticed that the turn read "most of us are not in the umbra and not in the clear," which grants that an umbra exists for Autistic bodyminds and that some people are in it — while spread 7 says of the ischaemic umbra that **nothing is coming back from there**. That is the write-off, and functioning labels and "profound autism" are how it is administered. The distinction is his and is now stated flat: **there is an umbra of *support*, which is a fact about an arrangement somebody built and can unbuild, and there is no umbra in an Autistic bodymind at all.** The argument runs off the optics already on spread 3 rather than off the medicine on spread 7: **an umbra is a relation between a source, an obstruction and a place, never a property of the ground it falls on** — the pavement does not *have* an umbra, it is *in* one, and nothing about the pavement is different. **Verified:** "profound autism" was proposed in **The Lancet Commission on the future of care and clinical research in autism** (2021), co-chaired by **Catherine Lord**, for Autistic people who are minimally or non-speaking, need 24-hour care and are **"not able to advocate for themselves"**; the **European Council of Autistic People** published an open letter calling the term highly problematic. **Steven K. Kapp**, "Profound Concerns about 'Profound Autism': Dangers of Severity Scales and Functioning Labels for Support Needs," *Education Sciences* **13(2), 106 (2023)**, open access — finds that minimally speaking Autistic people **can communicate**, especially with responsive partners, need testing that lets them demonstrate strengths, and **can self-advocate**. That is what makes the Commission's criterion partly a statement about the listener, the method and the room, and it is the spread's load-bearing claim. **Helen Edgar has written on this and is credited on the page**, quoted from *"Profound autism" does not exist — rather there are autistic individuals with PMLD / PIMD* (autisticrealms.com): "Autistic people with profound and multiple learning disabilities and health needs are not 'Profoundly Autistic,' they are autistic with specific needs." **Her position is represented as she states it** — the objection is terminological, she does *not* deny high support needs, and she is explicit that people with PMLD/PIMD require specialised multiagency support. The spread says the same on its face, because **refusing the umbra while denying anyone's support needs would be its own way of writing people off**, and the refusals set now carries that as its own entry. **Companion, not duplicate:** the *not a line* argument (a spectrum heard as a ranking, no norm star) is [No. 21]'s and is deliberately not re-walked; this spread is its companion, saying *and not an umbra either*. **Deliberately not claimed:** that functioning labels are settled in clinical practice, or that the zine resolves the Commission's stated aim of directing services to underserved people. **A renumbering bug caught before it shipped:** inserting spread 11 and renumbering the tail low-to-high made the second substitution match the value the first had just created, swapping the guardrail and the colophon — ids stayed unique so `check-markup.mjs` passed it clean, and it was found by printing the ids in document order instead of trusting the script. **Render checks (measured, not assumed):** contrast **745 elements, 0 screen failures, 0 print failures** — the page aliases the `--sp-*` tokens rather than hardcoding hexes, and the cover numeral was `aria-hidden` from the start. Overlap **691 text boxes, 0 collisions, 0 clipped**. Markup (incl. nav placement and the badge/card rule), sitemap and both directions of the prev/next chain clean; 91/91 badges. **Two figure defects found by eye at render size that no gate in this repo can see**, the figures having been extracted and re-rendered at 820px: Figure 2's two leader lines were **floating stubs pointing at bare penumbra** rather than touching the track and the disc edge, and its caption sat on the circle's lower edge; and Figure 4 carried a dashed spoke to the centre plus a solid radius to the core, which at render size **turned the core into a pie slice** — reading as a proportion the figure does not claim. Both fixed and re-rendered; source comments record why. `check-overlap.mjs` passed the page in both states, correctly — text over *artwork* is explicitly outside its scope. **One search-index diff investigated rather than rebuilt over:** after the Figure 2 fix the index reported STALE with an identical record length; the divergence was located at char 1576 of `#spread-4` and proved to be the two legend entries **swapping document order**, same character multiset. Legitimate, not a race. |
| 52 | We Learned to Build the Eclipse | 2026-08-15 | VERIFIED (five open items) | First check, done during the build. House zine, 21 spreads, *How We Got Here* (**eighth chain**). **Proposed, revised on the research, and the revision is the finding.** The pitched spine was *the obstruction is the instrument* — and it **breaks at link 4**, because Lockyer never went to the eclipse and Janssen stopped needing one the morning after his. The chain was rebuilt on the arc that actually holds: *wait for an obstruction you cannot control → learn what it revealed → build it yourself.* That also **closed a moral trap that prose would otherwise have had to fight**: "the obstruction is valuable" slides directly into *disabled people exist to reveal the flaws in your building*, and the corrected arc refuses it structurally, since the whole point is that you **stop needing the eclipse**. Recorded here because the first outline was wrong in a way worth publishing, and spread 18's second refusal states it on the page. **Verified:** the eclipse of **18 August 1868**, Janssen at **Guntur**, India; the anomalous yellow line near the sodium D lines detected at that eclipse by **several observers** — variously credited to Janssen, Georges Rayet, C. T. Haig, Norman Pogson and John Herschel — which is the first thing the popular sentence gets wrong. Janssen applied the no-eclipse method successfully **the following day, 19 August**. **Lockyer** conceived the same approach in **1866**, was delayed for two years by the want of an adequate instrument, and succeeded **20 October 1868**; both reports reached the **Académie des Sciences** within days and a medal was awarded to both — **for the method**. **The load-bearing contested claim (spread 7):** *neither report singled out the yellow line as significant.* Only Lockyer later designated it **D3** and proposed, with **Edward Frankland**, an element named for *hēlios*. **The 27 years (spread 8):** many chemists declined it — not because the spectroscopy was faulty but because **no sample could be produced**, nineteenth-century chemistry resting on substances that could be weighed, dissolved and combined. **Ramsay** isolated helium from **cleveite** on **26 March 1895** while pursuing argon, and sent a sample to Lockyer. **The phantoms (spreads 9 and 11):** **Ira Bowen (1927)** attributed the chief nebular lines to forbidden transitions of doubly ionised oxygen rather than "nebulium"; **Walter Grotrian (1939)** identified the coronal red line at 6374 Å as a forbidden transition in highly ionised iron and **Bengt Edlén (1942)** the green line and others, ending "coronium" — and the implied million-kelvin corona is how the coronal heating problem arrived. **1919:** eclipse of **29 May**, Príncipe and Sobral, under Astronomer Royal **Frank Watson Dyson** with **Eddington** at Príncipe; the Sobral **astrograph** plates excluded after losing focus, most plausibly through rapid temperature change during totality. **Earman & Glymour (1980)** argued the selection was theory-driven. **Kennefick** (incl. "Testing relativity from the 1919 eclipse: a question of bias," *Physics Today*) establishes that attributing the selection to Eddington is **not historically accurate** — the Sobral reduction was Dyson's team's at the RGO — and that agreement with Newton would have required assuming a **large change of magnification in addition to** the loss of focus; a **1979 re-reduction** with modern astrometric methods supported the 1919 treatment. **Lyot** invented the coronagraph c. **1930** and obtained the first photograph of the corona outside an eclipse at **Pic du Midi on 12 July 1931**. **Deliberately not quoted:** Lockyer's much-reproduced remark on watching Ramsay's sample glow in the discharge tube — widely repeated, not reachable in a primary source, so spread 10 **describes the event and prints no quotation**. **Flagged as ours, not the literature's:** the "one in three" framing on spread 9 is an editorial characterisation of three specific proposals, not a claim about the method's general success rate, and the colophon says so. **Joints: 12 documented, 4 contested, 1 leap**, counted programmatically from the `.joint-line` tags on the spreads — **the most contested joints of any chain in the collection**, which is a fact about the subject (a smoothed retelling, a quarter-century disagreement about admissible evidence, two elements that were never there, and an accusation answered after sixty years) rather than about sourcing. **A colophon error caught by the generator:** the draft claimed "eleven documented, three contested, one leap" and listed only three contested joints, omitting the phantom elements; the spine script's own tally disagreed with the prose, which is what surfaced it. The script now counts the first node — which has no incoming edge and so was silently excluded — **so the tool's number and the number a reader can count off the spreads are the same number.** **Collection page updated and re-counted from the pages, not remembered:** eight chains now carry **74 documented, 20 contested, 7 leaps**, reconciling exactly with the previous 62/16/6 plus this zine's 12/4/1. **A pre-existing error fixed in passing:** `collection-how-we-got-here.html` said "6 pieces" while carrying seven cards, and now says eight. **The spine is generated but structural, and the colophon explains why it is not a data plot like No. 50's** — the chain's dates carry no plottable quantity, and on a true linear year axis the four 1868 nodes fall within about two pixels. Generation is retained because the six chains before No. 50 drifted their geometry across hand-edited copies, and this one has 17. **Render checks:** contrast **0 screen, 0 print** failures; overlap **1007 text boxes, 0 collisions, 0 clipped**; markup, sitemap and both directions of the chain clean. **One cover fault found by eye at render size and fixed twice:** the motif's corona streamers reached x=250 and the ring ran under both the ghosted numeral and its "How We Got Here" label; the art was pulled back to x ≤ 200 and then nudged again when the label's first word still grazed the ring. No gate here sees text over artwork — the same fault as No. 48 and No. 51. **Open (1):** the 1868 accounts, the medal, and the claim that neither report flagged the yellow line come from **history-of-astronomy secondary literature, not from the Comptes Rendus themselves**; spread 7 is marked `contested` partly for that reason. **Open (2):** Bowen 1927, Grotrian 1939 and Edlén 1942 are carried from standard solar-physics histories, **not read at their own papers**. **Open (3):** the Kepler pinhole material (that pinhole solar diameters came out systematically too large, and that Tycho's correction was empirical) is from the history-of-optics literature; the 1604 book itself was read at the primary for No. 51 but **not searched for this claim**. **Open (4):** Lyot's date and priority are from observatory and encyclopaedia sources rather than his own papers. **Open (5):** spread 16's starshade and transit material is **deliberately generic** — no mission is named, because mission status changes and a dated claim would rot. **Extended 2026-08-15 after Ryan asked for more payoff, and the request found a real fault.** The turn ended at spread 17 calling the built condition an *accommodation* — which is exactly the word [Stimpunks' own page](https://stimpunks.org/2022/01/28/accommodations-individualized-responses-to-structural-design-problems/) exists to criticise: *Accommodations: Individualized Responses to Structural Design Problems*, where the title is the argument. The zine had collapsed three states into two. **Two spreads added** rather than one, because the material would not fit in one: **18, *Build it in, or keep asking*** — wait / ask / build, with the middle row named as the trap, quoting our own page twice (“We do not need more after-the-fact accommodations awkwardly bolted on exclusionary systems” and “Structural redesign is not about eliminating all accommodations, but embedding them so deeply that they become expected rather than exceptional”), and landing on the coronagraph's occulting disc as *where the instrument's precision comes from* rather than a concession; and **19, *Somebody poured it themselves*** — **Angela Glover Blackwell**, “The Curb-Cut Effect,” *Stanford Social Innovation Review*, **Winter 2017**, with the Michael Pachovas passage and the “equity is a zero sum game” sentence quoted verbatim from the article, plus Berkeley's first official curb cut on Telegraph Avenue in **1972**. **A second smoothed retelling caught and corrected on the page:** the famous story of activists smashing kerbs with sledgehammers and dynamite before dawn is **disability lore, not documented history** — what happened was Pachovas and friends pouring cement one evening. A chain that spends spread 7 on a smoothed retelling could not close on one. **Stress cases** paraphrased, not quoted, from Meyer & Wachter-Boettcher, *Design for Real Life* (A Book Apart, 2016) — the line usually attributed to that book could not be traced to it and is therefore not printed. **And Ryan's second note reframed the transfer itself, correcting something factually loose.** The draft called the eclipse "a rare and uncontrolled event" and described access as arriving "by an alignment nobody planned" — but **eclipse tracks were computed years in advance, to the minute and to the mile**, which is precisely why expeditions could be funded, crated and shipped to arrive on the right morning. The difficulty was never uncertainty. It was a **structural condition**: the observation existed only *there* and only *then*, and the entire cost of closing that distance — ocean, freight, years of planning, weather risk — was charged to whoever needed the observation. Spreads 3, 17 and 18 were rewritten on that basis, the three states renamed **reach it / ask for it / have it**, and the closing line changed from "manufacture of a condition that would otherwise depend on luck" to **"relocation of a condition — out of a sky nobody can reschedule, into an instrument on a bench."** This is a better map to the disability case than the luck framing was: the accessible venue across the city and the clinic with a nine-month list are not bad luck, they are conditions sited somewhere else with the travel billed to the person who needs them. **The guardrail on spread 19 is load-bearing and was written before the rest of the spread:** the curb-cut effect is an observation about what built-in conditions do, **never the justification**, and the page says the reason to cut the kerb was sufficient without a dividend for anybody else. **Spreads 18 and 20 deliberately carry no joint tag** (elaboration and guardrail respectively) so the tally is not double-counted; the curb cut **is** a new documented node and was added to the spine, taking the chain to 17 nodes. **The generator caught this too:** after the insert, the prose carried 18 tagged joints against 16 spine nodes, and `tools/gen-chain-spine.mjs` gained a `--rebuild` mode plus an explicit spread→node reuse map so the two can no longer drift. **And a silent no-op was caught by checking rather than trusting:** the renumbering script's fix to the colophon's “Spread 18 exists to refuse” cross-reference matched nothing, because it was anchored to a `<strong>` that is not there — found by grepping the result instead of assuming the edit landed. |
| 53 | The Moon Is Always Half Lit | 2026-08-15 | VERIFIED (three open items, one claim cut) | First check, done during the build. House zine, 14 spreads, *Star Stuff* register — one settled geometric fact carrying the belonging claim. **Searched before proposing, and the search decided the piece.** No. 17 *The Nearest Body* already owns the Moon, but it owns its **gravity** — tides, nearness beating mass, co-regulation; the **phases** were untouched, and light is a different physical story from mass, so this is the companion rather than a duplicate. Nos. 48, 51 and 52 use the Moon only as an **occluder**; none of them looks at the lit side. SKS returned **nothing from Helen Edgar on the Moon**, which settled guest-vs-house before a title was offered. **VERIFIED — the four principal phases are instants, quoted not paraphrased.** The US Naval Observatory states that “the phases New Moon, First Quarter, Full Moon, and Last Quarter are defined to occur when the excess of the apparent ecliptic (celestial) longitude of the Moon over that of the Sun is 0, 90, 180, and 270 degrees, respectively,” that these times “are instantaneous and well defined,” and that the crescent and gibbous phases are intermediate, “each of which lasts for about a week between the primary phases.” **Spread 4's entire argument rests on that asymmetry** — four knife-edges and four week-long stretches drawn as eight equal boxes — which is why it is quoted rather than characterised. **VERIFIED — Luna 3:** launched 4 October 1959; first photograph of the far side at 03:30 UT on **7 October 1959** from ~63,500 km; **29 photographs** covering ~70 percent of the far side; film developed onboard and negatives scanned and radioed back; the striking feature was the near-absence of maria (NASA / NSSDC). **VERIFIED — libration:** ~59 percent of the surface visible from Earth over time, via libration in longitude (orbital eccentricity against uniform rotation) and in latitude (axial tilt ~6.7° to the orbital plane). Spread 7 says “from Earth,” **not “tonight,”** deliberately — 59 percent is an accumulated total, not a single-moment fraction. **VERIFIED — sidereal 27.32 d, synodic 29.53 d**, the gap because Earth advances ~27° along its own orbit during one sidereal month, so the Moon must travel ~29° past a full circuit (~2.2 d) to restore the alignment. Both are mean values; Figure 4 is schematic and the colophon says so. **OURS, COMPUTED, AND SHOWN — the 50.2 percent grace note.** No source is cited because we did the arithmetic: for a sphere of radius *r* lit by a larger sphere of radius *R* at distance *d*, sin α = (*R* − *r*)/*d* and the lit fraction is (1 + sin α)/2; with *R* = 695,700 km, *r* = 1,737.4 km, *d* = 1.496 × 10⁸ km that gives ≈ **50.23 percent**. Wikipedia's solar-terminator article corroborates the *principle* for Earth (“a little over one half of Earth is illuminated at any point in time”) but **not the lunar number**, so it is presented as computed rather than sourced, and the colophon prints the formula so a reader can check it. The load-bearing claim on spread 2 is that **one hemisphere is always lit**, which needs no decimal. **PLAUSIBLE, and marked as such on the page — Mästlin's priority.** That **Michael Mästlin** was the first to *publish* an explanation of earthshine rests on a **single strong secondary source** (Thony Christie, *The Renaissance Mathematicus*); Wikipedia's Maestlin article does not mention earthshine at all, and we did not reach a primary text. Graded PLAUSIBLE, not VERIFIED. **VERIFIED — Leonardo:** correct explanation c. 1506–09 in the *Codex Leicester*, never published, and **wrong about the mechanism** (he credited Earth's oceans; most of it is cloud). Spread 9 states the error rather than tidying it. **VERIFIED — Mästlin taught Kepler** at Tübingen (Kepler studied there 1589–1594 and learned Copernican astronomy from Mästlin's lectures). **CUT DURING THE CHECK, and this is the entry's main lesson.** The draft wanted to claim that **Kepler credited Mästlin for earthshine in *Astronomiae pars optica* (1604) — the same book in which he coined *penumbra* for No. 51.** It would have been a perfect joint between two adjacent zines, **which is exactly why it was distrusted**. What the sources actually support is that Mästlin's 1596 *Disputatio de Eclipsibus* is “often referred to” in Kepler's 1604 work — *referred to*, not *credited for this*. That is an inference, not a citation, and per CLAUDE.md a search summary is a lead. The spread now claims only the documented relationship (teacher and student, either side of the same problem), and the colophon **publishes the cut** rather than quietly dropping it. **ALSO NOT CLAIMED:** any publication date for **Paolo Sarpi**, whose earthshine writings are variously dated c. 1578–83 and variously described as published or not; we could not settle it, so he is not on the page. **Cross-links, all deliberate:** No. 17 for tides and co-regulation (named and *not* re-walked, since this zine is about light); No. 21 for the spiky profile; No. 48 for the unseeable-is-not-unreal guardrail; No. 51 for the umbra distinction the guardrail borrows rather than re-derives — *a phase is a relation between a source, a body and an observer, never a property of the body*. **The guardrail is load-bearing and was written before the turn.** The easy version of this zine — “the Moon is always half lit, so the difference is imaginary” — is false and would be cruel to hand anybody; a crescent genuinely delivers less light to you. Spread 12 says so first, and the first refusal in the “Not:” set is **“that the hard days are not hard.”** **Render checks:** contrast **0 screen, 0 print** failures across 339 screen and 300 print elements including 25 SVG labels; overlap **655 text boxes, 0 collisions, 0 clipped**; markup clean (1,034 tags, 36 ids), badge and collection card added in the same pass, chain verified in both directions. **All four diagrams were inspected by eye at render size**, since no gate here can see that a drawing means the wrong thing — Figure 1 was checked specifically to confirm every one of the eight Moons is drawn lit on the same sunward side, which *is* the finding rather than a simplification. **Open (1):** the USNO text was read at its public FAQ page, not at the *Astronomical Almanac*'s own definitions. **Open (2):** Luna 3's figures come from NASA/NSSDC summaries rather than the original Soviet mission reports. **Open (3):** *wet moon* is given as folk usage for the low-latitude bowl crescent, with no cultural claim attached; the geometry is certain, the name's provenance is not researched. **Extended 2026-08-15, same day, after Ryan pointed at one of his own posts — and it replaced an invented example with a real one.** Spread 11 originally landed the turn on a made-up scene (a person at 3pm in an open-plan office versus at home on Saturday). Ryan surfaced [*R-word and Genius: Situationally Mute Hyperlexic Autistic*](https://stimpunks.org/2022/09/19/r-word-and-genius-situationally-mute-hyperlexic-autistic/) (stimpunks.org, **19 September 2022**), his own first-person account, which is strictly better on three counts: it is real and first-person from inside the community, it *names the variable* the geometry leaves abstract, and its own phrasing — **“depending on who was in the room with me”** — is literally the observer term in the angle, stated by the person being measured. **A new spread 12 was added** to carry it (guardrail → 13, colophon → 14, total 14), and the invented office scene was cut from spread 11 rather than kept alongside. **Read at the primary, not through a summary:** the post was pulled in full from the source and every quoted phrase on the spread is verbatim — *locked my lips*, *a semblance of psychological safety*, *Can I go now?*, *During my school career, I was both r-word and genius*, *the difference in impressions is due to psychological safety, it’s presence and absence*, *from being a full time r-word to a part time one, depending on who was in the room with me and if I felt safe enough speak*, and *Autistic people are psychological safety barometers*. **The slur is written “r-word” throughout, matching the source**, which is a deliberate stimpunks.org convention rather than our own softening. **One inference is flagged as ours, not his:** Ryan wrote the barometer sentence; the observation that it *inverts the direction of measurement* — a barometer's reading being a fact about the surrounding air, so the instrument was reading the room and the room recorded the result as a fact about the child — is this zine's addition and is marked as an interpretation. **Two terms credited to their originators rather than absorbed:** *exposure anxiety* is **Donna Williams**, *Exposure Anxiety — The Invisible Cage* (Jessica Kingsley, **2003**), Williams herself Autistic; *psychological safety* as a research construct is **Amy Edmondson**, “Psychological Safety and Learning Behavior in Work Teams,” *Administrative Science Quarterly* **44(2):350–383 (1999)** — a study of **work teams, not Autistic children**, so the colophon states plainly that **the application to classrooms and to Autistic people is Ryan's, not Edmondson's**. *Situational mutism* is used rather than *selective mutism*, following the source and the Stimpunks glossary, because *selective* implies a choice that is not being made. **A seventh refusal was added at the same time, and it guards the new spread against itself:** *not that psychological safety is the only variable* — spread 12 is one person's account and names safety because that was decisive **for him**, while sensory load, processing time, energy, pain, interoception and plain exhaustion are angles too. “Just make them feel safe” would be a new flattening. **And the colophon draws the line the other way as well:** spread 12 is **not offered as evidence for the optics** — it is a life the optics happen to describe well, which is a smaller claim. **Deliberately out of scope:** comparative claims about non-Western lunar phase systems (not researched, and not gestured at), and the calendar problem — that 29.53 will not divide 365.24 — which spread 10 names and then stops, because it is a *How We Got Here* chain rather than a zine. Ryan chose that scope explicitly when the piece was proposed, and it is **parked in `DECISIONS.md`** rather than half-told here. |
| 54 | How Anything Gets Built | 2026-08-16 | VERIFIED (one open item) | First check, done during the build. House zine, 8 spreads, filed in *Star Stuff*, crediting Helen Edgar throughout. Built from [The Five Neurodivergent Love Locutions](https://stimpunks.org/2022/01/22/the-five-neurodivergent-love-languages-2/), the most-read page Stimpunks has published. **The attribution chain was corrected mid-build by Ryan Boren, and the correction matters because our own pages disagreed.** Helen Edgar's Autistic Realms post says penguin pebbling was *"as far as I know"* first introduced by Amythest Schaber; her practitioner guide cites Stimpunks (2022), which cites Schaber — so every route led back to our own page. Ryan, who ran the page that put the framework into currency, gave the actual chain: **Schaber originated the Five Neurodivergent Love Languages; Stimpunks popularized them and put them into currency; the term *penguin pebbling* reached us later, through Lindsey (*brainsandspoons*).** A first draft credited all five names to Schaber and was wrong. *Locutions* for *languages* is Stimpunks' own substitution. **The primary was not missing — it was in the blockquote.** A draft colophon said Schaber's first post was unlinked and asked a reader to send it; Ryan pointed out that the 2022 Stimpunks page quotes and links the original thread directly: [neurowonderful, 27 May 2021](https://x.com/neurowonderful/status/1398061897086226432) (date confirmed from the post ID). Now cited in the colophon and the source list. *The search had been for a citation the source page already carried — read the page before searching around it.* **Open item:** Helen's page attributes the *term* penguin pebbling to Schaber, which conflicts with the chain above; flagged to Ryan for More Realms rather than corrected here, since it is her page. **Physics verified at primary or authoritative secondary:** Fraunhofer (1814–15) solar absorption lines; Kirchhoff & Bunsen (1859–60) spectral lines as elemental fingerprints; Payne (1925) stellar hydrogen dominance; El-Badry, Rix & Heintz, *MNRAS* **506**, 2269 (2021) — 1.3 million wide binaries from Gaia eDR3, separations to 1 pc; Li & Zhang, *MNRAS* **396**, 2176 (2009) — energy transfer in W UMa contact binaries, ΔT ≲ 200 K at q ≈ 0.3; Crawford (1955) resolution of the Algol paradox by mass transfer, numerically confirmed by Paczyński (1971); Lambrechts & Johansen, *A&A* **544**, A32 (2012) — pebble accretion named, giant-planet cores in 10⁴–10⁶ yr, and the pressure-bump self-limit at 10–20 M⊕; standard solar model central pressure ≈ 2.4 × 10¹⁶ Pa (**stated as "over 200 billion atmospheres" rather than a precise figure**, because published model values range from ~120 to ~265 billion atm and the spread is real). Grandin (1992) deep touch pressure; spoons are Christine Miserandino's (2003); the five-locution illustration is Betsy Selvam's. **The structural risk is named on the page rather than in this file.** Five clean mappings is exactly the shape [Too Good to Check](too-good-to-check.html) catalogues, so spread 7 says so outright and refuses the naturalistic fallacy in the same breath: *the physics does not justify the love; a universe that could approve could also decline; down to is not because of.* **Deliberately out of scope:** everything No. 46 already owns — the barycentre, tidal deformation and the three-body problem — and No. 46's correction that most stars are single. This zine takes only the wide end of the binary distribution and says so in its colophon. |

D., & Healy, S. D. (2011), "Individuality in nest building: Do Southern Masked weaver (*Ploceus velatus*) males vary in their nest-building behaviour?", *Behavioural Processes* **88**(1), 1–6, doi:10.1016/j.beproc.2011.06.011 — the full PDF was read, and **every statistic on spreads 5 and 6 is taken from the paper rather than from a summary of it**: seven colour-ringed males in southern Botswana, 21 nests, video coded blind to bird and date; no measured behaviour repeatable within a male during the initial attachment phase; two males biased right (PY, OR) and two left (BL, GM) with three showing none; **no bias across males** (*G* = 0.06, *n* = 161, *p* = 0.81 for IA; *G* = 0.00, *n* = 439, *p* = 0.96 for R); dropping rate falling as more nests were built (*F*₂,₁₂ = 7.53, *p* < 0.01); stronger laterality predicting a faster ring phase (*F*₁,₁₁ = 8.46, *R*² = 0.25, *p* = 0.01). **The authors' own hedge is carried onto the page rather than dropped:** they attribute the change "most readily … [to] increasing dexterity" and state that any role for cognition requires further work, and spread 6 says exactly that. **Verified at abstract level, and marked as such on the zine's own colophon:** Tello-Ramos, M. C., Harper, L., Tortora-Brayda, I., Guillette, L. M., Capilla-Lasheras, P., Harrison, X. A., Young, A. J., & Healy, S. D. (2024), "Architectural traditions in the structures built by cooperative weaver birds," *Science* **385**(6712), 1004–1009, doi:10.1126/science.adn2573 — 43 white-browed sparrow weaver groups, architecturally distinct roosts, consistent across years, differing between groups metres apart, not explained by weather, body size, tree height or relatedness, and robust to immigration. The full text is paywalled (science.org returns 403); the abstract was read at the St Andrews research portal and the study's findings confirmed against **lead author Tello-Ramos's own account** in *The Conversation*, 24 October 2024. **Cited through a primary rather than from it:** Collias, E. C., & Collias, N. E. (1964), "The development of nest-building behavior in a weaverbird," *The Auk* **81**(1), 42–52 — the "experienced birds build neater, more tightly woven nests" result reaches us **inside Walsh et al.'s discussion**, and spread 6 prints "reported in Walsh et al., 2011" rather than implying we read the 1964 paper. **Gray-Hammond sourcing, and one citation that had to be repaired:** the room passage on spread 2 and the *state of chaos* quotation are from *AuDHD and me: My nesting habits*, 1 July 2023, **read at source and live** at neurohubcommunity.org, where his writing now lives — Ryan Boren supplied the new host mid-build after the original davidgrayhammond.co.uk URL returned **HTTP 410**. The lilipadding definition on spread 9 is from *Lilipadding for Autistic People*, 7 June 2025, also read at source. **The essay *Autistic Nesting as the Physical Architecture of Lilipadding* is the one source not readable at its origin** — the Substack URL 410s — and its passages (*architecture of continuity*, the nest as the physical design of the lilypad, burnout as the collapse of the architecture, compassion as environment) are quoted from the Stimpunks glossary entry, which reproduces them with attribution. **That dependency is printed on the zine's colophon rather than only here**, because a source a reader cannot open is a fact about the citation, not a detail to bury. **The naturalistic fallacy is refused on its own spread, not in a footnote.** Spread 8 states four refusals: not permission from nature (*down to is not because of*; a universe that could approve could also decline), not instinct versus learning, not a clever-animal story, and — the one that costs something — **not every mess is a nest**, since Gray-Hammond himself describes burnout collapsing the architecture, and the ruins are indistinguishable from the doorway. The zine's positive claim is deliberately narrow: what the findings kill is the inference that *an order you cannot read is not an order*, which is a claim about observers rather than about what nature endorses. **Diagram fault caught by eye, not by a gate.** The first draft of the spread-5 chart ran the "all seven pooled" label straight through the PY bar and stacked two axis lines; `check-overlap.mjs` passed it, correctly — it does not measure text over non-text, and says so. Rebuilt with the finding on its own row and a single cyan dashed zero line, then re-checked at render size. Bars are drawn after Walsh et al.'s fig. 2, with solid fills for the four tested side-preferences and dashed outlines for the three without, and the caption names the figure. **Two quotations added on Ryan Boren's suggestion after the first build, and placed to mark where the analogy stops.** From the Stimpunks glossary [Niche Construction](https://stimpunks.org/glossary/niche-construction/), kept there as a pair under "A Space of One's Own" and gathered by Ryan in [Comfortable Niche Construction](https://boren.blog/2020/08/10/comfortable-niche-construction/) (10 August 2020): **@willaful**'s *"It took me decades to learn how to change my environment to make myself comfortable"* and **@steve_asbell**'s *"It took decades to learn I was allowed."* Wording verified against the glossary page. **Two things deliberately not asserted.** A web-search summary described the second as a reply to the first and dated the exchange 3 August 2020, which conflicts with the 10 August post that gathers them; the reply relationship is therefore **not claimed** and the pair is presented as the glossary presents it. And **no real name is attached to `@steve_asbell`** — the same search surfaced both an Autistic author-illustrator and a film executive of that name, so the handles are cited as published. **The placement is the argument:** the weavers have the first sentence in their data and nothing corresponding to the second, because permission is not a feature of nature but something people grant or withhold — which is the half that can be changed by deciding to. **Open:** (1) *Science* (2024) full text unread — abstract plus lead author's own account only; (2) Collias & Collias (1964) not read at source; (3) the Gray-Hammond nesting essay quoted from the Stimpunks glossary rather than from a live original; (4) Nair's *The Language of School Design* is cited from the passage quoted in the Stimpunks glossary, not read in the book (Thornburg, by contrast, is read at primary); (5) the sole attribution of that nesting essay to Gray-Hammond is inferred from its first-person-singular prose and the glossary's attribution, not from a byline we could read — and given the Adkin correction above, it should be confirmed with him. | 56 | The Noise Floor | 2026-08-17 | VERIFIED (four open items) | First check, done during the build. House zine, 15 spreads, *How We Got Here* — the ninth chain, ten documented joints, one contested, one leap. **Read in full at the primary, not from summaries:** H. Nyquist, "Thermal Agitation of Electric Charge in Conductors," *Physical Review* **32**, 110–113 (July 1928). Every Nyquist quotation on the page is verbatim from the paper: *"a universal function of frequency, resistance and temperature and of these variables only"*; *"no assumption has been made as to the nature of the two conductors. One may be made of silver and the other of lead, or one may be metallic and the other electrolytic"*; the footnote **"See preceding paper."** in full; and *"The average power, transferred from each conductor to the line in the frequency interval dν during the time interval l/v is therefore kTdν"* — which is **kTB stated in the primary**, not a modern gloss on it. Equation (1), E²dν = 4RkTdν, is his. **The spread-4 thermodynamic argument is his too, condensed rather than invented:** the resonant-network step by which a band-wise inequality would violate the second law is on p. 110–111, and the zine's "a silent resistor would be a perpetual-motion machine" is our compression of it, marked as reasoning rather than quoted. Also noted and *not* used: Nyquist's equation (8) gives the Planck-form quantum correction hν/(e^{hν/kT}−1), twenty years before it mattered — cut for space, logged here so a later piece can have it. **C. E. Shannon**, "A Mathematical Theory of Communication," *Bell System Technical Journal* **27** (1948) — **Theorem 17 quoted verbatim from the paper**, together with *"It is not possible to transmit at a higher rate by any encoding system without a definite positive frequency of errors."* Shannon writes C = W log (P+N)/N; **the page prints his form first and names the familiar B log₂(1 + S/N) as the same statement rearranged**, rather than quietly substituting the textbook version for the one he wrote. **A. A. Penzias & R. W. Wilson**, "A Measurement of Excess Antenna Temperature at 4080 Mc/s," *Astrophysical Journal* **142**, 419–421 (1 July 1965) — read in full. 3.5 ± 1.0 K; 6.7 K total at zenith of which 2.3 ± 0.3 K atmospheric and 0.8 ± 0.4 K ohmic; the aluminium-tape seam test; back-lobes measured with a ground transmitter at **ten** positions in both polarisations, giving < 0.1 K; and the parenthesis the zine leans on — *"isotropic, unpolarized, and free from seasonal variations (July, 1964-April, 1965)"*. **That parenthesis is the source for "ten months," which replaces the folk "about a year"** — the paper dates its own persistence and no secondary account was needed. Companion: Dicke, Peebles, Roll & Wilkinson, "Cosmic Black-Body Radiation," same volume, 414–419, dated 7 May 1965. **The pigeon story is corrected against its primary, and the correction is the zine's best beat.** R. W. Wilson, "The Cosmic Microwave Background Radiation," Nobel lecture, 8 December 1978 — read in full. Verbatim: *"A pair of pigeons was roosting up in the small part of the horn where it enters the warm cab. They had covered the inside with a white material familiar to all city dwellers. We evicted the pigeons and cleaned up their mess, **but obtained only a small reduction in antenna temperature**."* The retelling everywhere makes the pigeons the punchline; **the primary says the floor survived them**, and the clause that says so is the one that gets cut. Two further repairs from the same reading: **the famous phrase "white dielectric material" does not appear in the lecture** (grepped for; Wilson wrote "a white material familiar to all city dwellers"), and **the pigeons are not in the 1965 paper at all** — they arrive thirteen years later. Also from the lecture, and the reason rung 3 and rung 4 are the same apparatus rather than sequential: the travelling-wave masers were *"cooled to 4.2 K or less by liquid helium"*, the reference termination was an absorber in liquid He (twenty litres, ~twenty hours per fill), and Wilson states the principle himself — *"A fundamental limit to the sensitivity of a radiometer is the fluctuation in the power level of this noise."* **The rooms rung was checked expecting a leap and found to be documented, which changed the chain's shape.** The signal-to-noise framing is **not ours and not borrowed from Shannon by us** — it is in the standard's own explanatory literature. From the Acoustical Society of America's *Classroom Acoustics for Architects*, quoted verbatim: an *"average differential between speech level and background level (called signal to noise ratio) of about 15 dB, which means the A-weighted background noise level should not exceed about 35 dB"*, and the design instruction *"Rather than boost the instructor's voice through electronic amplification … we have to work to keep the background noise level inside the unoccupied classroom from exceeding 35 dB."* Reverberation limits of 0.6 s / 0.7 s by enclosed volume are from the same document's worked examples against ANSI/ASA S12.60 table 1. **Flicker:** A. J. Wilkins, I. Nimmo-Smith, A. I. Slater & L. Bedocs, "Fluorescent lighting, headaches and eyestrain," *Lighting Research & Technology* **21**(1), 11–18 (1989) — 43–49% modulation depth at 100 Hz on switch-start/choke circuits against under 7% on a ~32 kHz electronic ballast, double-blind cross-over, headaches and eyestrain *"more than halved."* **The caveat on the page is the author's own, not a hedge we added:** Wilkins' co-authored 2010 IEEE PAR1789 review states the effect *"is attributable to a minority that is particularly affected"* — which is the spiky-profile point arriving from the lighting literature rather than from us. **Offices:** Jungsoo Kim & Richard de Dear, "Workspace satisfaction: The privacy-communication trade-off in open-plan offices," *Journal of Environmental Psychology* (2013) — full text read from the eScholarship copy. Sound privacy is the highest dissatisfaction anywhere in the survey: **59% high-partitioned cubicles, 58% low-partitioned, 49% no/limited partitions, against 18% in private offices**, and *"Satisfaction with 'ease of interaction' was no higher in open-plan offices than in private office"* — i.e. the thing open plan is sold for did not appear. **The transfer is marked twice and the disanalogy is on the page, not down here.** Spread 12 states plainly that auditory filtering is not thermal noise, that there is no *k* for a nervous system, and that claiming the physics *proves* anything about Autistic hearing would be the naturalistic fallacy with a formula attached — then marks the joint `contested`. Spread 13's closing analogy is marked `leap` and says in its own body text that thermal noise entails nothing about who deserves a quiet room, that the moral claim stands on its own feet, and that what the physics supplies is narrower: it removes an excuse. **Endpoint:** Anna Remington & Jake Fairnie, "A sound advantage: increased auditory capacity in autism," *Cognition* **166**, 459–465 (2017) — a **capacity** account, in which one mechanism produces both the superior detection and the greater distraction, which is neither a deficit nor a superpower reading. Stimpunks' *Sensory Trauma* glossary entry already carries the "higher perceptual capacity" framing, so the endpoint is in-house rather than imported. **Sensory Trauma** is credited to **Autism Wellbeing**, who named it (*Sensory Trauma: Autism, Sensory Difference and the Daily Experience of Fear*), with the "hiding in plain sight" phrase quoted as the glossary reproduces it. **SKS sweep before proposing:** nothing of Helen Edgar's on noise or sensory floors — her sensory writing is low-demand parenting and energy management — so this is a house zine and she has not reviewed it. The sweep also confirmed no prior art in the repo on noise, Shannon or Nyquist; the CMB appears in **No. 8**, `why-difference-comes-first-zine` and `difference-first-frame`, but **always for its anisotropy** (difference comes first), never for discovery-as-noise, so this is a companion rather than a second telling. **The spine is schematic and the page says so** — unlike No. 50's, which plotted real TAI−UTC data. It draws a floor rather than measuring one; the geometry is generated by a script rather than hand-drawn, jagged where a rung is a measurement and smooth where a rung is an argument. Claiming plot-hood for a drawing would be the exact vice this collection was built against. **Two sources arrived from Ryan Boren mid-build, out of the SKS inbox, and one of them changed the argument.** **María del Carmen Rosas-Pérez**, *Disabling Acoustics: Impact of daily life acoustic environments on neurodivergent, auraldivergent and noise sensitive people*, PhD thesis, Heriot-Watt University, June 2026 (supervisors Laurent Galbrun, Mary Stewart, Sarah Payne) — read from the thesis PDF. Three studies: interviews with 12 UK Autistic adults; a mixed-method survey of **311 noise-sensitive participants** (59% neurodivergent, 44% reporting hearing differences); and **AcoustInA**, an Acoustical Inclusivity Assessment tool tested by 13 experts across 21 spaces. From §5.2.5, printed on spread 12: 87% of all respondents cannot filter out background sound or struggle to; **96% of Autistic and 94% of neurodivergent respondents against 77% of neurotypical ones, with no Autistic participant reporting it easy** (χ²=25.08, *p*<.001, V=.29). **The caveat is printed on the page in the same breath as the number, because without it the figure is an overclaim:** every one of the 311 was already noise-sensitive, so this is a difference measured *inside* a sensitive sample and is not a statement about Autistic people in general. **The finding that actually reshaped the zine is §4.3.3, "No habituation, but accumulation effect."** Spread 12 had been arguing that the circuit analogy *holds*; it now argues that this is precisely where it **breaks**, and breaks informatively — thermal noise is stationary (kTB is the same in the tenth hour as the first) while the measured human pattern is cumulative fatigue and distress. *A resistor never gets tired of being warm.* **That is a better joint than the one it replaced**, because a marked analogy that names its own failure mode is doing the work the notation exists for. It also produced a fifth refusal on spread 13: desensitisation is not merely ineffective but runs the wrong way. *Sensory gaslighting* is the term her reflexive thematic analysis develops for the dismissal participants reported since childhood, and **Participant 10's sentence now opens spread 13 in place of an objection we had invented** — a real testimony beats a rhetorical one. **Cited through her rather than from it, and marked as such on the page:** the exposure hypothesis behind "you'll get used to it" is **Formby et al. (2003)** — five participants, *none noise-sensitive*, two pure tones, two weeks — reported as she reports it, **not read by us**; and "sonic torture" for desensitising therapies is attributed by her to **Botha et al. (2021)** and is not presented here as our phrase. That n=5 detail is the collection's own subject arriving in the endpoint: careful measurement generalised far past what it can carry, then handed back to us as a character note. **Hari B. Srinivasan**, "The TRACE Framework: Improving Reporting of Sensory, Communication, and Contextual Factors in Autistic Physical-Health Research" (2026), doi:10.1177/25739581261477976 — read from the article; the quoted clause about being unable to determine whether outcomes reflect the procedure or the conditions is its own. **TRACE earns one paragraph rather than a spread**, and the reason it earns any is a parallel the chain had already set up without noticing: Penzias and Wilson's paper is largely *a published noise budget* (2.3 K atmosphere, 0.8 K ohmic, back-lobes < 0.1 K), and reporting the conditions of a measurement is unremarkable in radio astronomy — while physical-health research involving Autistic adults is still being asked to start. **Neither source was in the index when searched**, which is why the first SKS sweep missed them; they sit in the SKS `inbox/` and are not yet part of any indexed collection. **Open:** (1) **Johnson's own 1928 paper was not read in full** — cited for its page range (97–109) and priority on Nyquist's word, and the zine's description of his apparatus is standard history rather than his text; (2) **ANSI/ASA S12.60 itself is paywalled and unread** — every classroom figure comes from the Acoustical Society's own explanatory publication, which quotes the standard, and the colophon says so on the page; (3) **Wilkins et al. 1989 was read at the abstract**, not the full paper, so the modulation percentages and "more than halved" carry that limitation, and the sample size is not printed because the abstract does not give it; (4) **Remington & Fairnie 2017 was read at the abstract**, which is a second reason the joint is marked contested and is stated in the colophon; (5) **Formby et al. (2003) and Botha et al. (2021) are cited through Rosas-Pérez's thesis and not read at source**, which the colophon states; (6) the Rosas-Pérez thesis is **a doctoral thesis rather than a peer-reviewed journal paper** — examined, and the figures are printed with their sample caveat, but it has not been through journal review and the page should not be read as if it had. All six gates clean: markup (0 problems across 116 pages, 101/101 collection badges), sitemap (116 entries, 0 problems), chain (100 pages, no prev/next mismatches), contrast (0/510 screen, 0/467 print, 69 SVG labels, 6 gradient-clipped unmeasured), overlap, and the search index rebuilt at 15 records / 100% coverage. **One real fault the gates caught:** `check-overlap.mjs` found the Figure 4 "×8" tick label sitting on the "signal power →" axis label, a 10×7 px collision at a shared y=124. | 57 | The Shell Was Your Calcium | 2026-08-17 | VERIFIED (five open items) | First check, done during the build. House zine, 10 spreads, four diagrams, filed in *Star Stuff*. Proposed by **Ryan Boren** from Essy Knopf's video, with the honest note that he was unsure a star stuff angle on eggs existed; **it does, and it is the calcium**. **The borrowed term is credited as unowned, which is the entry's main attribution decision.** *Egg* / *getting your egg cracked* is **trans community language** and no individual coined it so far as anyone has established. Traced via **Evelyn Bauer**, "Cracking the History of the Trans 'Egg'," *Them*, 5 December 2024 — read at source: earliest documented use is an Urban Dictionary entry for *egg mode* by user **bitplane7, 23 August 2016**; **r/egg_irl created 26 July 2017**; and **Susan Stryker**, quoted in the piece, cannot date her own first encounter with it though it felt "intuitively obvious" by 2019. **We print the origin as unknown rather than promoting the most citable name into a coiner**, which is [Too Good to Check](too-good-to-check.html)'s central failure and the trap recorded on `quillery.html` and in No. 55. *Use of a term is not provenance* — and neither is the earliest surviving write-up. The **extension of the term to Autistic and ADHD experience** is **Essy Knopf**'s (Autistic, ADHD, queer, disabled; LCSW), from *The "Egg Crack" Moment: Realizing You're Neurodivergent*, YouTube, **16 March 2025**, and the zine notes that **he credits the trans origin himself**, which many retellings do not. **His name is spelled from the channel's own page metadata, not from the transcript** — the auto-captions render it "Essie", and Ryan's handed-over transcript carried that artefact; a web search for the transcript spelling surfaced a plausible-but-unconfirmed match, so the `ownerChannelName` field was read directly. A small case of the standing rule: *the aggregator's spelling is a lead*. **Read at source, open access, and the source of every figure on spreads 4 and 5:** Halgrain, M., Georgeault, S., Bernardet, N., Hincke, M. T. & Réhault-Godbert, S., "Concomitant morphological modifications of the avian eggshell, eggshell membranes and the chorioallantoic membrane during embryonic development," *Frontiers in Physiology* **13**, 838013 (2022). **The zine's central finding is a pair of the paper's own results held side by side:** shell thickness between EID0 and EID15 shows **no significant difference (*p* = 0.110)** while breaking strength between EID11 and EID15 is **significantly reduced (*p* = 0.007)** — the wall is dismantled from inside without measurably thinning. Also from it: mineral solubilisation begins ~EID11 and is mediated by the chorioallantoic membrane; the eggshell supplies **more than 80%** of the embryo's calcium (**>90%** in altricial species) at roughly **2.21 g per egg**; and the embryonic skeleton holds **more than 80%** of the whole embryo's calcium between EID11 and EID15 and **~75% at hatch**. **The reading of "nobody could see it because there was nothing to see" as the masking parallel is ours**, and spread 4 marks it as a parallel rather than a result. **Read in full from the published article:** Mair, A. P. A., Gonzalez-Figueroa, M., McConachie, D., Goodall, K. & Gillespie-Smith, K., "Grief, Relief, and Belief: A Social Media Study on Late Identification of Neurodivergence," *Autism* **30**(5), 1344–1359 (2026), doi:10.1177/13623613261437916. **This is the paper that kills the door.** 731 posts gathered across seven platforms in March/April 2023 (Instagram, Reddit, TikTok, Tumblr, Twitter, WordPress, YouTube), of which **225** observed grief and relief in relation to late identification. The overarching theme is the **Grief, Relief, and Belief Cycle**, described by the authors as **oscillating** and as a "non-linear, evolving, and individual process"; the four themes are **(1) The Life I Could Have Had, (2) Grieving for My Younger Self, (3) Feeling Gratitude, (4) Post-Diagnosis Burnout**. The clause quoted on spread 9 — diagnosis as "a first step, rather than a terminal component of a service" — is theirs, from the abstract. **Theme 4 is the hedge the popular retelling files off**, and it is why the third refusal exists. Found by an SKS sweep before proposing, already in Ryan's library; the term "egg crack" itself returns **nothing** in the SKS corpus, which is what prompted verifying its provenance rather than assuming it. **Verified at primary, abstract level:** Vince, M. A., "Behaviour and aeration of the respiratory system in the domestic fowl embryo," *The Journal of Physiology* **263**(3), 465–474 (1976) — the three-stage lung-aeration sequence (major airways aerate, then respiratory movements begin, then tertiary bronchi aerate slowly) and the beak-clapping observation are quoted from the abstract and marked as such on the colophon. **The duration of internal pipping is deliberately not printed as a number.** Husbandry and incubation sources widely give 12–24 hours between internal and external pipping in domestic fowl, but **every scholarly route to a primary was paywalled** — Visschedijk (1968), *British Poultry Science* **9**(2), and the *Poultry Science* review both returned 403 — so spread 6 says "hours" and the colophon states the omission and why. This is the one figure the zine wanted and refused to take on husbandry authority. **Hatching synchrony, cited from abstracts, titles and contemporaneous reporting rather than from the papers:** Vince, M. A., "Social facilitation of hatching in the bobwhite quail," *Animal Behaviour* (1964) — embryos click in the last days; an egg incubated 24 hours behind a clutch accelerates and hatches with its siblings when the eggs are in contact. **The retarding direction is the finding the zine is built on and it is a real, separately published result, not an inference:** Vince, M. A., "Retardation as a factor in the synchronization of hatching," *Animal Behaviour* (1968), with "The retardation of hatching in Japanese quail" (1970) and "The synchronisation of hatching in quail embryos: Aspects of development affected by a retarding stimulus," *Journal of Experimental Zoology* **229**(2) (1984), the last testing 128 embryos in 33 groups with a **0.9 clicks/second** retarding stimulus. So synchronisation is bidirectional — advanced embryos are slowed as well as retarded ones accelerated. **The reading of that as mutual aid is ours and is flagged on the page as ours**, not Vince's. All three papers are behind publisher paywalls and the colophon says so. **Egg tooth and *musculus complexus*** are given as standard comparative anatomy with **no resorption timing printed**, because it varies by species: a temporary keratin projection, shed or resorbed after hatching, with a horny caruncle in turtles and crocodilians, a true egg tooth in squamates shed with the first skin slough, and both in the echidna (a recent primary exists: Hughes and colleagues on the echidna egg tooth and caruncle, *Developmental Biology*, 2022, not read here); the hatching muscle hypertrophies for the job and shrinks afterward. **Piezoelectric bone** is **Iwao Yasuda**'s (1954), with **Bassett & Becker**, *Science* **137**(3535), 1063–1064 (1962) — house-standard sourcing carried from earlier pieces, not re-derived for this one. **The naturalistic fallacy is refused on its own spread rather than in a footnote**, per [A Promise, Not a Finding](a-promise-not-a-finding.html): spread 8 states that chick embryos have no identities, closets or diagnoses, that a universe which could approve could also decline, and that *down to is not because of*. The fifth refusal is the one that costs something — **not the egg as the enemy** — because masking cost us enormously and also kept some of us alive, and the shell held humidity for twenty-one days before handing over its substance. **The first refusal is where the borrowing pays its debt:** Bauer's warning that naming somebody an egg can push them further in is carried verbatim in substance, and the biology happens to agree without being asked to — the egg tooth is on the inside of the shell, so *you can only click*. **Deliberately not duplicated:** `hatchery.html` already spends shell gas exchange (The Breathing Shell), piezoelectric collagen (The Collagen Egg), the Arrau turtle calling from inside its shell (The Arrau Egg), stellar onion layers, endosymbiosis and the leatherback's ossicles, and it credits the world-egg cosmogonies (Rigveda, Kalevala) rather than redrawing them. The Hatchery was checked spread by spread before writing, and **none of this zine's four findings — the calcium transfer, the strength-without-thickness result, internal pipping, or the clicking — is among them**; the Arrau gets a one-line hand-off on spread 7 rather than a second telling, and the cosmogonies are left entirely to the Hatchery. **Three diagram faults caught by eye at render size, none of them visible to any gate**, which is the standing lesson that a passing gate is not a correct drawing: the cover motif drew the interior as spine-and-ribs that **read as a fern or a sparkler** at 290px with a star sitting on top of the ribcage, and it also **duplicated the door mark's cracked-egg-with-stars-inside design** — redrawn as a single plain bone, which states this zine's argument and belongs to no other page; the spread-5 diagram had the same fern problem and now uses the same bone glyph, deliberately, with a source comment saying why; and the spread-6 diagram's airflow arrow **pointed the wrong way**, down from the lungs into the air cell, when the changeover being described runs the other way — now two arrows up from the air cell into the lungs, which also removed an accidental face. A fourth fix was cosmetic-but-misleading: the spread-4 "SAME WALL" leader line sat on the far side of the day-15 arc, pointing into empty space. All six gates clean: markup (117 pages, 0 problems, 102/102 collection badges across 13 collections), sitemap (117 entries, 0 problems), chain (101 pages, no prev/next mismatches in either direction), contrast (0/334 screen, 0/301 print, 13 SVG labels measured, 18 gradient-clipped unmeasured — the `.spectrum-word` spans, covered by the shared print block's `-webkit-text-fill-color` rules), overlap (650 text boxes, 0 collisions, 0 clipped), and the search index rebuilt at 10 records / 100% coverage. **Open:** (1) the **internal-pipping duration** is unverified at primary and deliberately unprinted — Visschedijk (1968) is paywalled at every route tried; (2) **Vince 1964, 1968, 1970 and 1984 were not read at source** — all paywalled — so the 24-hour catch-up, the retardation direction and the 0.9 clicks/second figure rest on abstracts, titles and contemporaneous reporting; (3) **Vince 1976 was read at abstract level only**; (4) the **echidna egg-tooth primary was not read**, and the comparative-anatomy claims are given as standard rather than from papers read for this zine; (5) **Yasuda (1954) has never been read at source on this site** — it is carried from earlier pieces on the strength of Bassett & Becker's citation of it, and that inheritance should be closed out once rather than re-inherited a sixth time. |
| 58 | The Stone You Keep | 2026-08-18 | VERIFIED (three declined claims, two open items) | First check, done during the build. **Guest zine, 10 spreads, eight diagrams, filed in *More Than Human*** — developed after **Helen Edgar** (Autistic Realms), *Stimpunks Solidarity and Otters* (2026), written from the Solidarity Sesh otter cam; JSON-LD author is Helen, following the No. 29 precedent. Rest framing follows Ryan Boren, *Infodumplings: Otters Already Know How to Rest* (Stimpunks, 2026). **Verified at primary:** Tinker, M. T., Bentall, G. & Estes, J. A., "Food limitation leads to behavioral diversification and dietary specialization in sea otters," *PNAS* **105**(2), 560–565 (2008) — diets broadly similar in the food-rich site, a behaviourally based dietary polymorphism in the food-poor one, with correlated behavioural suites and improved foraging efficiency on individually preferred prey. Law, C. J. et al., "Tool use increases mechanical foraging success and tooth health in southern sea otters (*Enhydra lutris nereis*)," *Science* (2024), doi:10.1126/science.adj6608 — 196 radio-tagged otters; tool-use frequency varies greatly between individuals and tracks diet specialisation (frequent users on snails and clams, non-users on urchins, crabs and small bivalves); access to larger/harder prey with **reduced tooth damage**, most strongly in females. Hall, K. R. L. & Schaller, G. B., "Tool-using behavior of the California sea otter," *Journal of Mammalogy* **45**(2), 287–298 (1964) — Point Lobos, six days, 30 instances; **the same stone frequently retained across successive food items**; mean **35.5** blows per mussel, range **6–88**, sd 20.4. Wright, T. J. et al., "Skeletal muscle thermogenesis enables aquatic life in the smallest marine mammal," *Science* (2021), doi:10.1126/science.abf4557 — basal metabolic rate ~**3×** predicted for body size, driven by thermogenic leak respiration in skeletal muscle, present already in newborn pups with immature muscle. Estes, J. A. & Palmisano, J. F., "Sea otters: their role in structuring nearshore communities," *Science* **185**(4156), 1058–1060 (1974) — Aleutian islands with and without otters; otter removal → increased herbivory → destruction of macrophyte associations (urchin barrens). Larson, S. et al., "Genetic diversity and population parameters of sea otters, *Enhydra lutris*, before fur trade extirpation from 1741–1911," *PLOS ONE* (2012) — ~**2,000** animals worldwide by 1911, ~**99%** loss of original abundance; the North Pacific Fur Seal Treaty of 1911 ended the commercial hunt. Pelage: Williams, T. M. et al., *Marine Mammal Science* (1992), and Kuhn, R. & Ansorge, H., on hair density in *Lutra lutra* and *Enhydra lutris* — densest measured mammalian pelage, total density ranging **26,413–164,662 hairs/cm²** by body region, i.e. roughly sixfold *within one animal*; the zine states the range rather than the headline maximum. **Three claims declined, and this is the substance of the check.** (1) **The lifelong favourite rock under the *left* forearm.** Traces only to aggregators — SpaceDaily, A-Z Animals, zmescience, thefactbase, HandWiki — every one of them agreeing, which is exactly the condition that should send us to the paper. Hall & Schaller support retention **across successive food items within a foraging bout**; no lifelong retention, no side named. **The pouches and the reuse are real; the romance is not established.** The zine states only the sourced version. *Note that the popular form appears in Helen's essay and in our own Infodumplings post* — settled with Ryan 2026-08-18 that the zine tells the verified version without issuing a correction against either, since neither argument depends on the embellishment and a reliable object returned to is precisely what the primary shows. **Logged here so the next otter piece does not reintroduce it.** (2) **Otters sleeping hand-in-hand as affection.** Rafting and kelp-anchored rest are documented; the hand-holding image is aggregator-level and its romantic reading unsupported, with the aggregators disagreeing among themselves about the mechanism. The zine uses the kelp and says on its face that it is leaving the story out. (3) **The number of otter species.** IUCN Otter Specialist Group and other standard sources give 13 vs 14; omitted entirely, as nothing in the argument needs it. **Open items.** (a) *Maternal transmission of diet specialisation* (mother → pup) is widely attributed to Tinker's group and was **not** primary-confirmed, so it is absent from the zine; worth chasing before any follow-up leans on cultural inheritance. (b) The commonly cited *~25% of body mass eaten daily* was not primary-confirmed and is not used — the metabolic spread rests on Wright 2021 instead. **Honesty notes on the page:** the Tinker result is a between-site comparison rather than a single population tracked through time; the diversification is a response to scarcity and the zine explicitly draws no moral from scarcity itself; and the closing states that the belonging claim does not rest on the otters — *a rhyme, not a proof*. Refusals set declines cute, superpower, rest-so-you-can-produce, be-a-keystone, the smoothed retellings, and animal-as-metaphor-for-people. |

The constellation renamings are interpretive, not factual astronomy claims. No corrections. |
| 2 | The Star Stuff You're Made Of | 2026-07-22; re-checked 2026-08-12 | CORRECTED → VERIFIED | **2026-08-12 — the iron entry carried an overclaim this ledger had already corrected elsewhere, and the sibling sweep never reached it.** The entry read "Everything heavier than iron on this list, **and most of the iron itself**, comes from that catastrophe," with `forge: 'the last fusion · supernovae'` and an Origin fact of "massive cores, supernovae" — i.e. core-collapse only. That is the same error fixed in **Zine No. 2 on 2026-08-03** against **Kobayashi, Karakas & Lugaro**, "The Origin of Elements from Carbon to Uranium," *ApJ* 900:179 (2020): roughly **half** of iron comes from **Type Ia** supernovae — white dwarfs accreting enough matter to detonate — not from core collapse. Corrected in all three places (forge line, Origin fact, and the notes paragraph, which now names both parents and cites Kobayashi et al.). **Why it survived nine days:** the 2026-08-09 sibling sweep *did* touch this file, but it was a targeted one-claim fix for the hydrogen-vs-helium slip and was recorded as such; nobody re-read the file for the *iron* correction from six days earlier. Two sweeps, two different claims, same file, and the second did not imply the first. **Sweep run for this correction:** every "iron" claim in the repo was re-read. The rest are generic — "forged in a dying star," "from stars and their deaths," "calcium and iron from supernovae" — and are all correct, since a Type Ia *is* a supernova and *is* a stellar death. The field guide's "most of the iron" was the only false statement. Sagan's "interiors of collapsing stars" is a verbatim quotation and is untouched. Original 2026-07-22 pass follows. All 8 elements' body-abundances and forge sites confirmed: H (Big Bang), O & Ca (massive stars / core-collapse SNe), C (red-giant triple-alpha; Hoyle resonance), N (CNO), Fe (last fusion → SNe), K (explosive burning), I (r-process / neutron-star mergers). |

| 5 | The Ways of Being a Star | 2026-07-22; re-checked 2026-08-14 | CORRECTED → VERIFIED | **2026-08-14 sibling re-check (from No. 46), and the original pass missed it because it checked the coordinate line rather than the prose.** The *Never Alone* entry asserted "**most stars live bound to another**" and closed on "*Most of the sky was never single.*" **Both are false** — Lada (*ApJ* 640, L63, 2006) finds two-thirds of main-sequence stellar systems in the Galactic disk are single, because multiplicity is a steep function of primary mass (~26% M dwarfs, 44 ± 2% solar-type, near-total at the top; Duchêne & Kraus 2013, Raghavan et al. 2010) and the galaxy is overwhelmingly M dwarfs. The entry's own `coords` field, "~half of Sun-like stars have a partner," was **correct all along**; the surrounding prose generalised from *Sun-like stars* to *stars* and inverted the answer — which is precisely the smoothing this collection exists to argue against, committed in our own text. Entry rewritten with the mass dependence stated and all three sources named, the closing line replaced, and a **dated correction note left on the entry's own face** rather than only in this ledger. Logged publicly in `changelog.html`. Original pass, unchanged and still verified: red-dwarf longevity; brown dwarf (~13–80 M_Jup, ~8% solar-mass fusion limit); Cepheids & Henrietta Leavitt's period–luminosity law; pulsars (to ~700/s); red giant, white dwarf, blue giant. Figures flagged approximate in-text. |
| 6 | Symbioses | 2026-07-22; re-checked 2026-08-03 | CORRECTED → VERIFIED | **2026-08-03 sibling re-check (from Zine No. 5):** "The Underground Exchange" entry said the network "can shuttle resources between them … in which a sunlit elder can feed a seedling shaded on the forest floor," and listed "a forest-wide network" and "chemical warnings" as what the partners exchange. Per Karst, Jones & Hoeksema (*Nature Ecology & Evolution* 7, 2023), the forest-wide reading, the seedling benefit and the defence-signalling are not established, and elder-to-offspring provisioning has no published evidence — the entry now states this plainly and keeps the settled root-to-fungus trade (~nine in ten land plants). Original pass: Helen Edgar fact-check applied. Fixed MLK/Baldwin misattribution (King, *Letter from Birmingham Jail*, 1963). Lewis: `PLAUSIBLE` — paraphrase attributions consistent with their published work; exact citable source not primary-confirmed this pass. |
