Rewritten to the shared-breath material No. 24 actually opens with. |
| Designing for Paper (print-design.html) | 2026-08-11 | VERIFIED (derivative) | Backing material in *Notes & Rationale*, split out of the Print collection page later the same day when measurement showed that page was carrying two jobs and burying its own collection (cards at 2,518px of 4,470px — 56% down, against ~27% on the sibling collection pages). **Every claim on it is about our own artifacts and tooling and inherits the Print collection row below**, which traced each one: the 190 × 259 mm intersection and the A4/Letter box arithmetic; the 9 mm-not-10 overflow; the 44-of-46 blank-print figure; `ls-broadside.html` at 3 sides on Letter / 2 on A4 and `six-ways-broadside.html` at 4 on both; Atkinson from the Braille Institute; Atkinson lacking `U+2605`; the five darkened ink ratios on white. **Two details appear here that the collection page never carried, and both were checked before being written:** the sub-700px breakpoint, verified against the comment in `six-ways-broadside.html` recording that a breakpoint above the A4 page box (192mm ≈ 726px) was matching *on paper* and silently clipping side B; and the star path's 0.382 inner-radius ratio, read from the broadsides' own stylesheet comment rather than restated from memory. **Held honestly:** the *What the tools cannot see* callout is framed as a standing caveat rather than a solved problem, and lists all three worked examples including the nested-anchor fault this page's own sibling shipped. |
| Collection page — Print | 2026-08-11 | VERIFIED | Sixth collection landing page, and the odd one out: the other five sort zines by **register**, this one sorts by **medium**, which is a different axis and the page says so rather than letting it read as a sixth zine collection. **It makes no claim about the world** — every factual statement on it is about our own artifacts and process, and each was verified against the thing it describes rather than restated from memory. Confirmed against the sheets and the tools: 190 × 259 mm; the A4 box at 9 mm margins is 192 × 279 and the Letter box 198 × 261; at 10 mm the Letter box is 259.4 mm against a 259 mm sheet, which is the sub-pixel overflow that produced a third page; the 44-of-46 blank-print figure (per `CLAUDE.md` and the 2026-08-08 pass); `ls-broadside.html` measuring **3 sides on Letter and 2 on A4** and `six-ways-broadside.html` **4 on both** before the redesign, per `tools/check-sheets.mjs`'s own recorded findings; Atkinson Hyperlegible Next drawn by the Braille Institute; Atkinson lacking `U+2605`. **The five ink ratios on the page were recomputed from the hexes for this page rather than copied from the sheets' comments** — gold `#92400e` 7.1:1, violet `#5b21b6` 9.0:1, pink `#9d174d` 7.9:1, green `#166534` 7.1:1, cyan `#164e63` 9.1:1, all against white — because a number restated across artifacts without re-measuring is mode 02, and this page restates five of them. **Held honestly on the page:** it states that the tools cannot see a drawing that means the wrong thing, and gives both worked examples (the Same Water figure's label sitting on its own line while passing all seven contrast checks; the edge stamp that printed through the middle of the L★S sheet). It also names `shorthand-evolution.html` as **not a broadside** — it fails rule four — rather than quietly counting it as a sixth, and publishes the reason the functioning-labels card does not exist (blocked on Helen Edgar). The four rules are Ryan's, given verbatim in the request that commissioned the page. **One layout fault shipped and was caught by Ryan looking at it, which is the page's own caveat landing on the page:** four card descriptions linked back to the piece each sheet compresses — `<a>` elements nested *inside* the `<a class="card">` wrapper. Nested anchors are invalid HTML, and the parser resolves them by closing the outer anchor early, so every affected card fragmented on screen: the bordered box ended after the moment line, the description became an orphan block, and the `.card-footer` (the *Print ready* tag and the arrow) detached and floated below. **Every automated check passed it** — contrast measured the text as legible because it *was* legible, the search index read the words because the words were all there, and `check-sheets` does not apply to a screen page. Fixed by dropping the inner links to plain emphasis, which is what every other card on the site already does; the sheets themselves link back to their parents, so no route was lost. Verified afterwards by counting `.card` children of the grid (6, each holding a title, a description and a footer, with no stray non-card children) and by a repo-wide scan for nested anchors, which now reports **zero across all 70 pages**. |
| Collection page — Easter Eggs (*The Turtle Moves* coda) | 2026-08-15 | VERIFIED — carries one correction | Added at Ryan's request: the **Didactylos passage** from *Small Gods* (1992), and a drawing of Great A'Tuin, the four elephants and the Disc, labelled *The Turtle Moves*. **The quotation was not retyped.** It is copied from <a>No. 35</a>, where it was checked against the book after shipping unconfirmed from aggregators — including **the italic on the final <em>Moves</em>**, which the plain-text versions circulating online drop. Same class of fault as the *Hogfather* typography at No. 49, and the reason the verified copy in the repo is now the source of record rather than a fresh transcription. **The correction the drawing owes, and the reason it is stated on the page.** A world-turtle carrying elephants carrying the world is **not Hindu cosmology**, though it is endlessly repeated as such. The stack appears in **John Locke**, *An Essay Concerning Human Understanding* (1690), as a story about &ldquo;an Indian,&rdquo; and is repeated by **Bertrand Russell** in *Why I Am Not a Christian* (1927); reference scholarship on the epics and major Puranas finds **no world-upholding elephant in them at all**, and von Humboldt attributed the confusion to Sanskrit *nāga* carrying both &ldquo;serpent&rdquo; and &ldquo;elephant.&rdquo; This agrees with what No. 35 already established independently — **Kurma is a pivot for churning, not a world-bearer** — so the two entries corroborate rather than repeat. What Pratchett is riffing on is therefore a *European* joke about India, and the page says so, which is also why the drawing is deliberately **not** filed beside Field Guide No. 10's five *turtles people made*: it is a novelist's invention drawn as one. **Verified:** the four elephants are **Berilia, Tubul, Great T'Phon and Jerakeen**; Cori Celesti at the Hub and the rimfall are Discworld's. **Open:** the elephants-not-in-the-Puranas claim rests on reference scholarship rather than a primary Sanskritist reading, and is phrased no more strongly than that. **Also corrected in this pass:** two present-tense claims of &ldquo;five checking gates&rdquo; — on this page and inside No. 49 — were stale from the day `check-overlap.mjs` landed as the sixth. Dated changelog entries still say five and are left alone, because they were true when written. All six gates run clean on both pages, including a print render checked by eye: the drawing needed a page-scoped override so the ground-filled occluders go white on paper and the far legs stay lighter than the near pair, which the shared block would otherwise flatten to one slab. |
| A Promise, Not a Finding | 2026-08-15 | VERIFIED (derivative) | Foundations essay, split out of **No. 49** at Ryan's read that the zine had turned out load-bearing. **The split was checked before it was made:** `grep` across the repo found the three governing ideas — the belonging claim as a promise, lies-to-children, and First Sight and Second Thoughts — occurring **only** in No. 49 and in the two pages that describe it. Three principles the rest of the site presupposes were reachable only through an easter egg deliberately kept off the front page, which is the argument for the essay and is recorded here because it was an evidence-based call rather than a taste one. **Derivative:** every quotation and every source is No. 49's, whose row above is `CORRECTED → VERIFIED`, and nothing here is asserted that is not asserted there. The two *Hogfather* passages are reproduced from the copy checked against the book on 2026-08-14 rather than re-transcribed, small capitals and the italics on *show*, *rightness* and *become* intact — the same discipline applied to the Didactylos passage on the Easter Eggs coda. Lies-to-children stays credited to **Cohen & Stewart** (*The Collapse of Chaos*, 1994; *Figments of Reality*, 1997), First Sight and Second Thoughts to **Pratchett** (*Wintersmith*, 2006). **The stated risk, and the guard against it.** An essay that states three borrowed ideas as house principles is one careless draft from laundering Discworld-derived thinking into &ldquo;our method,&rdquo; which is *precisely* the fault No. 49 exists to correct. So the credits sit above the argument rather than in a footnote, the closing refusal is **&ldquo;Not ours&rdquo;**, and the sources block separates what is borrowed from what is ours. **Rebuilt before shipping.** The first draft opened on "our masthead says you are made of star stuff and the universe loves you for it", inheriting No. 49's error wholesale; Ryan caught it and the essay was rebuilt on **LYDTYSS**, which is stronger, because that phrase has neither defect — the subject is us rather than the cosmos, and *down to* is extensive rather than causal. The essay therefore stops being a correction of the phrase and becomes an explication of it, and quotes the Bone Song colophon in full as the sentence that *did* get it wrong and repaired itself in three words. **New here, and ours:** the argument that a belonging claim resting on *variation persists in nature* would be **revoked by any paper finding it does not**, so a revocable claim was never doing the work — offered as reasoning, not as a finding; the grammatical argument that *down to* marks reach and not reason; the observation that the ladder carries a **moral** limit as well as an epistemic one, with Field Guide No. 10's five *turtles people made* as the case; and that **only Second Thoughts can be automated**, since no gate detects a beautiful pattern doing your looking for you. All six gates clean on the new page and the four it touched. Chain verified in both directions at 88 (inserted between *Who Is Holding the Candle* and No. 3); index card, collection card, badge and sitemap entry added in the same pass. **The Pratchett estate has no involvement and was not asked.** |
| Starlight | 2026-08-17 | VERIFIED — three open items | Foundations essay, newly authored. Two claim families, checked separately. **(1) The colour of the universe.** The retracted value is quoted **verbatim from the primary**: footnote 3 of the preprint, [arXiv:astro-ph/0110676](https://arxiv.org/abs/astro-ph/0110676) (Baldry, Glazebrook et al., *ApJ* **569**:582, 2002), text extracted from the PDF rather than from any summary — "we have computed RGB values of 0.269, 0.388, 0.342&hellip; the closest match in standard RGB color lists&hellip; is 'pale turquoise'." **The preprint still carries the uncorrected footnote**, which is what makes the correction checkable at a primary rather than only reportable. Glazebrook's two quotations ("It's our fault for not taking the color science seriously enough. I'm very embarrassed" / "I don't like being wrong, but once I found out I was, I knew I had to get the word out"), the white-point explanation, and the Munsell Color Science Laboratory's involvement are from the **Johns Hopkins University announcement of 13 March 2002**, *Color of the Universe Corrected by Astronomers*. **This is an institutional press release, not a paper, and our own [Too Good to Check](too-good-to-check.html) names "the press release instead of the paper" as one of six ways a fact goes wrong** — so it is labelled as a release on the page's face. The exception is deliberate and narrow: *there is no paper for the correction*, which was announced publicly before the journal version appeared, so the release is the primary artifact for the event rather than a substitute for one. The authors' own page (`pha.jhu.edu/~kgb/cosspec/`, cited in the preprint's footnote) **is gone** — the host redirects to a department homepage and returns 403 — so the release was read in ScienceDaily's reproduction of it, and that reproduction is the weakest link in this row. "A conditionally perceived shade of beige," the 200,000-galaxy figure, the ten-billion-year drift toward redder, and the naming contest (entries *skyvory* and *univeige*; winner *cosmic latte*) are from [NASA's APOD, 2 July 2002](https://apod.nasa.gov/apod/ap020702.html). **`#FFF8E7` is marked as a rung on the page itself**, in a callout: it is one sRGB rendering of a colour defined only relative to an observer — which is the authors' own point in the words *conditionally perceived* — over a 200,000-galaxy survey volume rather than every star, and it moves with cosmic time. **(2) Everything else.** **Brandeis** quoted from the primary and grepped directly, not from an aggregator: *Other People's Money and How the Bankers Use It* (1914), ch. V "What Publicity Can Do", first published *Harper's Weekly*, 20 December 1913 — full text, Project Gutenberg #57819. Two things the popular version drops are restored on purpose: the second clause ("electric light the most efficient policeman") and **Brandeis's own hedge, "is said to be"** — the most-quoted sentence in the literature of transparency is marked by its author as a borrowed proverb. **Papert & Harel** quoted verbatim from the primary essay text ("Situating Constructionism," introduction to *Constructionism*, Ablex, 1991) — "constructing a public entity, whether it's a sand castle on the beach or a theory of the universe." **Terry Pratchett**, *Wintersmith* (2006), First Sight and Second Thoughts: derivative, credited to [A Promise, Not a Finding](a-promise-not-a-finding.html) and No. 49, whose rows carry the check. [The Stimpunks Learning Space](https://stimpunks.org/space/) is cited only for what Stimpunks itself practices, which is the one claim our own site is primary for. **(3) The repository numbers** — 115 pages, 94,718 tags, 1,648 ids, 1,270 search records across 114 pages, 114 ledger rows, 21 dated changelog releases and 169 entries of which 50 are fact-checks, ~2,850 lines across the six gates — were **measured on 2026-08-17 by running the tools**, not recalled; the page says so and says they will drift. **(4) Internal history re-checked against the public record rather than restated from memory**, since this essay's whole subject is not trusting working notes: 44 of 46 pages printing blank, 90 elements across 45 pages of gradient-clipped text, the 196-entry unmeasured list, 629 records against 637, 110 contrast failures on the morning of 2026-08-13, the collision gate's seven first-run defects, and the 12% coverage page all confirmed in [changelog.html](changelog.html). **One discrepancy found and one draft claim cut.** The comment in `starstuff.css`'s print block records the gradient fault as "196 elements across 40 pages" where the changelog says **ninety elements across forty-five pages** and gives 196 as the length of the *unmeasured list* — two different numbers conflated in a code comment; the essay follows the changelog and the comment is flagged for repair. And a draft sentence claiming the front page "printed untitled for a month" was cut before shipping: the record supports the fault, not the duration. **Open items.** (a) The JHU release has not been read at the institution's own domain, only in reproduction — replace the citation if the original or an archive copy becomes reachable. (b) **Who spotted the white-point error is not named on the page.** The release confirms the Munsell Color Science Laboratory; a named individual appears only in secondary reporting, so the page says "colour scientists" and stops. (c) **The person who suggested "cosmic latte" is not named** for the same reason — the account is widely repeated and nowhere primary-confirmed here — and an early draft's "a man reading the newspaper" was changed to "somebody," since the gender came from the same unconfirmed reporting. **Self-referential check.** This is the first page on the site to state that generative AI facilitates the work; the three attribution errors it cites as examples (No. 49's "our masthead", No. 54's closed citation loop, Field Guide No. 14's missing byline) are each already logged in their own rows and in the changelog, and are described here exactly as those rows have them. Screen and print contrast measured (331 screen / 320 print elements, 26 SVG labels, 0 failures); collision, markup and sitemap gates clean; search index rebuilt. |

| It Take a Joyful Sound (joyful-sound-playlist.html) | 2026-08-11 | VERIFIED, with two items published as unconfirmed | Newly authored. **Every one of the 36 YouTube embeds was resolved through the oEmbed endpoint before shipping**, so each video ID is confirmed to be the song the card names — the failure mode on a page like this is a card whose embed silently points at something else, and it is invisible to every other gate we run. That pass also produced a correction: the Stimpunks PSA page lists *Shave the Pride* without an artist, and it is **Le Butcherettes** (2015). **Release years were taken from MusicBrainz and cross-checked, not from memory, and years that could not be pinned were simply omitted rather than guessed** (pink suits, Noun, Armstrongs, Tommy Concrete, Feral the Earthworm, Shungudzo, Dream Nails' *Take Up Space*). One year would have shipped wrong from memory: ***Keep On Livin'* is 2001**, from *Feminist Sweepstakes* — not 1999, which is Le Tigre's self-titled debut and does not contain it. **Punk history:** *Punky Reggae Party* recorded July 1977, produced by Lee "Scratch" Perry, issued in Jamaica on Tuff Gong and Black Art and later as the B-side to *Jamming*; written after The Clash's cover of Junior Murvin's *Police and Thieves*; namechecks The Wailers, The Damned, The Jam, The Clash, Toots & the Maytals, Dr. Feelgood. Rock Against Racism founded 1976 after Eric Clapton's Birmingham endorsement of Enoch Powell; the Victoria Park carnival on **30 April 1978**, ~100,000 people, six miles from Trafalgar Square via Cable Street, with The Clash, Steel Pulse, X-Ray Spex and the Tom Robinson Band on one bill and Misty in Roots leading the march. Riot Grrrl manifesto published in *Bikini Kill Zine 2*, 1991. The Linda Lindas at the Los Angeles Public Library, **4 May 2021**, AAPI Heritage Month, Epitaph deal within days. **Acoustics:** timbre as the composition of upper partials — Helmholtz, *On the Sensations of Tone* (1863); harmonic distortion as the product of a nonlinearity is standard signal theory; bone conduction and the skull's low-frequency weighting traced to Tobias Reichenbach's group at Imperial College. **Two things are published as unconfirmed rather than quietly asserted, and both are named on the page itself.** (1) **The Slits' removal from the *Punky Reggae Party* namecheck** — reported, reason given as their being women, traceable only to secondary sources; marked *reported* in the text. (2) **"Everything that was normally supposed to be hidden was brought to the front."** This circulates everywhere, including across stimpunks.org, as an unattributed quotation credited to "punk subculture." Traced: it is the Wikipedia punk-subculture article, where it appears as **unquoted prose** footnoted to **LeBlanc, 1999** — **Lauraine LeBlanc**, *Pretty in Punk: Girls' Gender Resistance in a Boys' Subculture* (Rutgers University Press, 1999). So the idea has a real scholarly source and **we cannot confirm the wording is LeBlanc's rather than an encyclopaedia editor's summary of her**. The page keeps the phrase, credits LeBlanc for the argument, and says the wording is unverified. **The house rhyme is fenced explicitly twice:** neither the harmonic-distortion parallel nor the piezoelectric-bone reach is offered as a mechanism, and the page states outright that no one has shown gig-level sound pressure produces a meaningful piezoelectric signal in the skeleton. **No lyrics are reproduced anywhere on the page** — every card describes its song, by decision, with the reason stated in the colophon. |
| Stimming and Belonging with AURORA (aurora-playlist.html) | 2026-08-11 | VERIFIED, with one open item on its parent zine | Newly authored, companion to No. 13. **All 28 video IDs resolved through the oEmbed endpoint before shipping** — the failure mode a card embed has is pointing silently at the wrong performance, and no other gate can see it. **Release years taken from MusicBrainz and cross-checked against the zine's own colophon, not recalled**, and every year on the page agrees with what No. 13 already published: *Runaway* / *Running With The Wolves* 2015; *Conqueror*, *Warrior*, *I Went Too Far*, *Through The Eyes Of A Child* 2016; *Queendom*, *It Happened Quiet*, *Forgotten Love*, *Gentle Earthquakes* 2018; *Animal*, *The Seed*, *A Different Kind Of Human*, *Mothership* 2019; *Exist For Love* 2020; *Cure For Me*, *Giving In To The Love* 2021; *Invisible Wounds*, *The Conflict Of The Mind*, *Some Type Of Skin*, *A Soul With No King*, *To Be Alright* 2024. **Auroral physics:** O ¹S → ¹D at 557.7 nm, upper-state lifetime ≈0.7 s; O ¹D → ³P at 630.0 nm, ≈110 s; both forbidden under electric-dipole selection rules; collisional quenching suppresses both at lower altitudes, so green appears ≈100–150 km and red only above ≈150 km. Confirmed against atmospheric-optics and auroral-spectroscopy sources, with altitudes stated as approximate on the page and in the figure. **The Galileo coinage of "aurora borealis" (1619) was deliberately cut** — it is repeated everywhere but the attribution is genuinely contested (the *Discourse on Comets* was published under Guiducci's name, and Gassendi is also credited), and the page needs only the uncontested etymology. **The house rhyme is fenced explicitly**, including the unusual step of naming which part of the analogy the physics does *not* license: selection rules are not social rules and quenching is a statement about collision cross-sections. **Attribution:** *stim listening* is **Jim Irion**'s coinage (via the Stimpunks glossary); *Stimming with Aurora* is Ryan Boren's playlist. **The quotation is condensed and says so** — the Red Bull session answer is spoken, so filler and false starts are cut with ellipses marking every cut; nothing added, nothing reordered. Note the source is a third-party re-upload of a Red Bull session rather than an official channel; it is the video already in Ryan's own playlist. **Two things deliberately withheld.** (1) **No diagnosis.** Aggregator and SEO pages (wellyhub, thenews.com.pk and similar) are attaching specific conditions to her name; those are not primary sources and the page names none, matching No. 13. (2) **We do not attribute stimming to her.** She describes her stage movement as emotional expression, not as stimming, and the page says on its face that the recognition is ours. **OPEN — on Zine No. 13, not on this page.** No. 13's colophon states she "spoke publicly for the first time about being neurodivergent" in November 2025. **We could not verify the "first time" claim.** No primary source for the late-2025 interview was reachable — only aggregators — and Wikipedia's article carries "She identifies as an introvert and neurodivergent" in a sentence whose only citation is a **2018** *Independent* interview, which leaves room for an earlier public statement. The new page therefore makes no first-time or date claim at all. **No. 13 should either cite the primary interview or drop "for the first time."** |

| Nobody Got His Eyes Right (bowie-playlist.html) | 2026-08-11 | VERIFIED, with one item marked as reported | Newly authored, companion to No. 12. **The page is built on a correction, so the correction was traced properly.** Bowie had **anisocoria**, not heterochromia: both irises the same colour, the **left** pupil permanently dilated after **George Underwood** punched him in **February 1962** and a fingernail scratched the eyeball, paralysing the muscles that contract the iris. Primary-ish source: Kevin Hunt, *The remarkable story behind David Bowie's most iconic feature* (*The Conversation*, 2016), which states explicitly that the different-coloured-eyes reading is an illusion produced by the fixed pupil. **Noted honestly: Hunt is a design and visual-culture academic, not an ophthalmologist**, so the page claims only what he claims, and **the impaired-depth-perception detail is marked as *reported*** — it comes from lower-quality secondary sources and Hunt does not make it. The figure is labelled schematic. **The constellation:** *Stardust for Bowie*, January 2016, **MIRA public observatory** (Belgium) with **Studio Brussel** — seven stars near Mars in the *Aladdin Sane* bolt. MIRA themselves described it as an invented asterism, and the **IAU's 88-constellation list was fixed in the 1920s and has not changed**. **The seven star names are deliberately not reproduced:** the lists in press coverage are internally inconsistent — one gives both "Spica" and "Alpha Virginis," which are the same star — so we cite the gesture and refuse the corrupted catalogue. **Ziggy's retirement**, Hammersmith Odeon, **3 July 1973**: the page says *most* of the band rather than the band, because Mick Ronson knew and by several accounts the stage manager and road crew did too. **This correction is made on the page itself**, in a callout, precisely because the surrounding essay is about anecdotes getting rounded up — it would be indefensible to do the same thing two sections later. **★** released **8 January 2016** (first-release date confirmed against MusicBrainz release-groups, not recalled), *Lazarus* video the day before, death **10 January 2016**; Tony Visconti's "parting gift" line is from his own public post of **11 January 2016**. **All 31 embeds resolved through oEmbed** before shipping. **Years came from album first-release dates via release-group queries, after a recording-level query returned useless reissue dates** — a catalogue this heavily repackaged reports 1990 as the "earliest release" for *Space Oddity* if you ask the wrong way, which is worth recording as a method note. No lyrics reproduced. **The page's own refusals include the two symmetrical errors it could have made:** damage-as-source-of-art, and calling the eye iconic and stopping. |

| Search | 2026-08-08; tooling fixed 2026-08-11, 2026-08-17 | VERIFIED (no claims) | **2026-08-17 — the remainder record was gated on a coverage percentage, so the pages it failed were the ones that scored best.** Found by **Ryan Boren**, by grepping `search-index.json` for a distinctive sentence from a collection page's intro prose rather than trusting the coverage figure the builder prints beside that page. *Tidal force really does fall off with the cube of distance* returned **0**. The chunker claims a page on its repeating unit — on `collection-star-stuff.html` the 23-card grid — and step 3c then indexes whatever no chunk claimed; that step ran only when coverage fell under `COVERAGE_FLOOR` (0.9). **This is the `about.html` lesson wearing a higher number.** A percentage is a ratio, and the thing 3c protects is an *absolute quantity of prose*: the larger the repeating unit grows, the more text the same percentage conceals, so the gate leaked worst where the number looked healthiest and leaked as a cliff rather than a slope. **Measured, not assumed** — three pages sat above the floor with real prose unclaimed: `collection-star-stuff.html` 93% / **2,532 chars** (the curatorial argument, which is the entire reason a collection page exists rather than a folder), `index.html` 97% / **4,484** (every `.collection-intro` section blurb on the landing page), `changelog.html` 100% / **1,422** (its intro and colophon). **8,438 characters unfindable.** The reported defect was one page; the sweep found the class. **The diagnosis turns on why the other twelve collection pages were fine:** they sit at 20–84%, drop through the 3b heading segmenter, and had their intros indexed all along — `collection-star-stuff.html` crossed 90% only by *growing* to the largest grid on the site, so the page was penalised for succeeding, and Kin (84%) and Star Gazing (79%) would have lost theirs silently on the day they gained a member. **Fixed by removing the gate, which costs nothing it was ever protecting:** 3c can only append text no record claimed, so it could never double-index, and `push()`'s 40-char minimum is the correct filter because it measures prose rather than ratio. The leftover is now segmented by heading where it has headings — the front page's thirteen section intros land on `#collections`, `#field-guides` and the rest instead of one 4.4KB record that deep-links nowhere — with the single-lump path kept as the fallback, so `inclusion-safety-creed.html` (stray text around one `<h1>`) is unchanged at 7 records. **The second half was our markup, not the tool:** twelve of the thirteen collection pages had `h2.section` headings with **no `id`**, against the house rule, so even indexed sections could only be linked by `#:~:text=` guess; `collection-easter-eggs.html` was the one page that had done it right, and its pattern (id + `scroll-margin-top: 5rem`) is now on all thirteen — **46 ids**, hand-picked where a mechanical slug would truncate mid-phrase, since a fragment is a URL somebody can hand on. **Verified:** the three predicted pages changed by the predicted amounts and **no other page moved** (1,315 → 1,334 records); `--check` passes immediately after the build, so the generator is still deterministic; every `#id` fragment in the index resolves to a real `id` in page source, the only exceptions being the 198 JS-rendered field-guide entries that by design exist only after render; the search UI was driven end-to-end against a local server, returning `collection-star-stuff.html#sorted-by-register-not-by-date` and `index.html#field-guides` for prose that had returned nothing; `scroll-margin-top` measured resolving to exactly 80px at the target. All six gates pass. **No gate could have caught this, including this one:** a missing paragraph is not a colour, a tag tree, a collision, a page count or a sitemap entry — and the one instrument that could see it was printing **93%** in a column where 100% was ordinary. Earlier entries follow. **2026-08-11 — the generator was non-deterministic, and the failure mode was silence.** `build-search-index.mjs` waited a flat 1300ms after `Page.enable` and then read the DOM, i.e. every build raced a stopwatch. Observed in the wild during this session: one run wrote **629 records / 936,594 chars** where the runs either side gave **637 / 951,741** — eight records and 15KB missing, **exit 0, no warning** — which is precisely the shape this file keeps warning about, a truncated artifact that is indistinguishable from a healthy one. It also made `--check` useless: byte-comparison reported STALE seconds after the generator itself had written the file, so the ship gate cried wolf and, worse, had already passed a truncated build. **Fixed by waiting on a condition instead of a duration** (`readyState: complete` plus text length steady across two samples) **and then extracting twice and requiring the reads to agree** — the agreement is the only step that actually proves nothing was captured mid-render. A page that never stabilises now throws. **Deliberately not gated on `document.fonts`:** the extractor reads `textContent`, so webfonts cannot alter a single captured character, and gating on them hung the run for nine minutes on a Google Fonts request that never resolved — a correctness gate on something that cannot affect correctness. **Plus a per-page regression guard:** any page producing fewer records than the committed index is printed, and a page producing **none** exits non-zero and refuses to overwrite, since a 1.25% repo-wide dip is far below what any global threshold would catch. `--force` overrides for genuine deletions. **Verified rather than assumed:** three consecutive builds now byte-identical; `--check` passes immediately after a build (the original symptom); the guard tested by feeding it a page that renders empty, which correctly refused the write and left the index untouched; the index rebuilt after the test is byte-identical to the one before it. Build time fell to ~56s from a floor of ~86s of dead sleep. No indexed content changed. **And the new guard immediately earned its keep by catching a bug introduced the same hour:** `tools/check-sheets.mjs` had been written with `--remote-debugging-port=9411`, which is `build-search-index.mjs`'s port — `check-contrast.mjs` already carried a comment about deliberately taking 9412 to avoid exactly this. Running the three gates back-to-back, as the ship routine does, let the index build attach to a browser the sheet checker was still shutting down, and a page came back with **zero records**. Under the old code that would have been written out silently; instead it aborted and named the page. `check-sheets.mjs` moved to 9413, with the reason recorded in the source, and the three gates now run twice in sequence clean. Original entry follows. A tool, not an artifact. Makes no claim about the world: its only factual assertions are about itself, and each is measured — 425 records over 47 pages, index 175 KB gzipped, and the query staying client-side (there is no network call after the index fetch; verified in the network log). **It asserts nothing about the content it surfaces** — snippets are quoted verbatim from the indexed pages, so every claim in a result inherits the status of its source row. `search.html` is excluded from its own index. One limitation worth recording: the accent fold is 1:1 by design, so ß does not fold to "ss" and searching "Knauss" will not find "Knauß". |
| Design System | 2026-08-08 | VERIFIED | Newly authored. Every factual claim traced to a primary source or measured directly from the repo. **Typeface:** Atkinson Hyperlegible released free by the **Braille Institute** (2019), named for founder **J. Robert Atkinson**, designed for letterform distinction for readers with low vision; **Atkinson Hyperlegible Next** released 2025, adding five weights, a variable format, and 150+ language support — all confirmed against brailleinstitute.org/freefont (primary). **Palette lineage:** the accents are **Tailwind CSS v3's 400 ramp**, confirmed hex-exact against `tailwindlabs/tailwindcss` v3.4.17 `src/public/colors.js` (primary source, not the docs site, which now publishes OKLCH) — violet-400 `#a78bfa`, pink-400 `#f472b6`, amber-400 `#fbbf24`, cyan-400 `#22d3ee`, green-400 `#4ade80`, gray-50 `#f9fafb`; converting our hexes to OKLCH reproduces Tailwind v4's published hue angles to three decimals. **Solarized:** the sixteen values and the stated design goals ("precise CIELAB lightness relationships"; "reduces brightness contrast") confirmed against ethanschoonover.com/solarized (primary, Ethan Schoonover). **`grep` over every `.html`/`.css`/`.svg` in the repo returns zero Solarized hexes, and `git log -S` finds no mention in any commit** — so the "began as Solarized" origin rests on Ryan's recollection of pre-repository work and is stated on the page as recollection, credited for method only, never as a traceable derivation. **All numbers computed, not asserted:** CIELAB L\* and WCAG contrast ratios for all 17 `--sp-*` tokens (D65, sRGB); accent L\* spread 64.6–80.7; body text 18.84:1 vs Solarized dark's 4.75:1; `--sp-dim-soft` published as a failure at 3.27:1, then fixed. Typeface counts (44 Atkinson pages, all on Next after the 2026-08-08 migration; 3 Fraunces-set pages) and the print measurements (44 of 46 illegible before the fix; 47 of 47 after) counted from the repo and verified by print-media emulation plus pixel sampling. **Google Fonts availability verified by request, not assumption:** Next publishes wght 200–800, so the `200..900` range every Next page had been requesting returned HTTP 400 with zero `@font-face` rules — corrected to `200..800` and confirmed by watching the woff2 download. The migration's no-reflow claim is measured: 286.97px → 286.78px advance on a fixed test string, 0 of 126 probed text blocks changed height, width or line count. **Contrast claims re-measured after remediation:** `--sp-dim-soft` lifted #63607a → #807d98 (3.27:1 → 4.97:1) and six further AA failures fixed, each verified against its own composited background rather than the page ground; all 47 pages now pass AA on screen. |
| Collection pages (Star Stuff · Star Gazing · More Than Human · Kin · Stars We Grew Up On) | 2026-08-11 | VERIFIED (derivative) — one open item | Five newly authored curatorial landing pages. **They make no new claims about the world.** Every scientific claim is a one-line restatement of a claim already traced in the rows above, and each inherits that row's status: star-forged calcium (No. 1); mitochondria as former free-living bacteria (No. 7); the early universe's near-uniformity needing tiny perturbations to make any structure (Nos. 6, 8, and the CMB material in *Difference-First Frame*); tidal force falling off with the cube of distance (No. 17); relativistic clock rates and the GPS correction (No. 19); water holding and then flipping at a threshold rather than stiffening by degrees (No. 20); the electromagnetic spectrum as one continuous field (No. 25); Umwelt and the tick's three signals (No. 15); niche construction (No. 16); ethodiversity per Tarragnat (Nos. 8, 27); the octopus's distributed nervous system and the jellyfish's nerve net (No. 27); crab-like body plans evolving at least five times and being lost at least seven (No. 31); chondrichthyan cartilage as a **secondary loss** rather than a primitive state, per Brazeau et al. 2020 on *Minjinia turgenensis* (No. 32); Rozin & Fallon (*Psychological Review*, 1987) on disgust as a food emotion at root with largely learned objects (No. 33); Greenland shark maturity at about 156 years, Nielsen et al. (*Science*, 2016) (No. 34); Deleuze on identity produced out of a prior field of difference (No. 11); the rhizome, Deleuze & Guattari 1987 (No. 26); *wyrd* as the Old English word for becoming (No. 28). **One claim of my own was caught and corrected before shipping:** a first draft of the Star Stuff page wrote that "nearness beats mass by a factor of 27 million," which conflates the Sun-to-Moon **mass ratio** with the tidal advantage — the ratio of tidal effects is roughly 2.2:1 in the Moon's favour, a completely different number. Rewritten to state the mass ratio and the cube-law separately, which is how No. 17 already had it. **House hedges carried, not dropped:** the Star Gazing page states on its face that relational quantum mechanics is one interpretation among several rather than consensus; the More Than Human page names the contested part of the mycorrhizal story explicitly, citing Karst, Jones & Hoeksema (*Nature Ecology & Evolution*, 2023) on common mycorrhizal networks and the absence of published evidence for elders preferentially feeding kin (Nos. 5, 26, FG 6); the Star Stuff page's refusals block names the naturalistic fallacy and points at No. 9's bell-curve-to-eugenics warning. The Stars We Grew Up On page makes no scientific claim at all — it characterizes Nos. 12 and 13 and refuses the superpower framing. **Open:** the Star Gazing page quotes **Helen Edgar** verbatim on what star gazing means to her ("awe inspiring and not definite … possibilities of connecting things and ideas"). The words are hers, relayed by Ryan from an informal message, and lightly normalized for print (hyphens to em dashes, a comma at a line break). Needs Helen's sign-off before it stands as a public verbatim quote, or downgrade to a marked paraphrase. |
| Too Good to Check | 2026-08-11 | VERIFIED (derivative) — one fresh primary | Newly authored Foundations essay, and the reader-facing front door to *this file*. **It is almost entirely derivative by design, and that was the scoping decision:** the piece covers only misconceptions this collection has actually worked, so every one of its seventeen receipts restates a correction already traced in a row above and inherits that row's status — the mycorrhizal networks (Nos. 5, 26, FG 6), the Sagan compression (No. 6), Orion's Belt (No. 9), MLK/Baldwin (FG 6), the Eno/Kelly *scenius* wording (No. 31), neurodiversity's collective origin and the Walker/Chapman *pathology paradigm* attribution (Nos. 9, 3), crip time (No. 19), Galton 1883 (No. 9), carcinization's seven losses (No. 31), shark cartilage and the secondary-loss skeleton (No. 32), "dirt is matter out of place" (No. 33), the turtle scapula and "turtles all the way down" (No. 35), the press's "adopted" dolphin (No. 36), and the distaste/disgust correction (No. 33). A page arguing for primary sources has no business making a claim it has not itself traced, so it makes none. **One source checked fresh at the primary for this piece:** Carol Black, "Science / Fiction: 'Evidence-based' education, scientific racism, & how learning styles became a myth," carolblack.org — fetched and read rather than summarised, and both quoted fragments are verbatim from her page: "the claim there is scientific consensus where there is no such thing, that an open, ongoing area of scientific inquiry has reached a settled conclusion" and "as irrational and 'anti-science' as a climate denier." **The page carries no publication date and the essay says so** rather than inventing one. Note this is a *different* Black item from the `PLAUSIBLE` quote at No. 8, which remains open and is named as open on the page. **Milton (2012), Fricker (2007) and Charlton (1998)** carry their existing statuses (Nos. 15, 25, 30; No. 9; No. 3). **The title is handled as an instance of its own mode one:** "too good to check" is a journalism maxim of unestablished origin, variously attributed, so the references section states that we use it unattributed and why — rather than picking the most plausible-looking attribution, which is the exact error the essay is about. **Held honestly, on the page:** it states that these are not the seventeen most important misconceptions in circulation, only seventeen we can show full working on; that several rows rest on live rather than closed findings (the chondrichthyan secondary-loss reading, Rozin & Fallon as one theory of disgust among several, and Karst et al. as an argued position in an active dispute — **explicitly refusing to drop that hedge in the sceptical direction either**); and it publishes three currently-open ledger items by name, including the No. 8 Black quote, the paywalled Webster→Deleuze line at No. 29, and the five live instances of the altered Eno wording on stimpunks.org. **Refusals** name "do your own research," science literacy as a gate, the pleasure of the debunk, contempt for people who repeat the story, and any claim to be finished. **Credit:** Helen Edgar is credited by name on the page and in the colophon for both corrections that are hers (MLK/Baldwin; distaste vs. disgust). **Render checks (measured, not assumed):** 354 text elements composited against real ancestor backgrounds, zero AA failures on screen; under emulated print 346 elements, zero failures; 32 SVG `<text>` nodes clear on both. Mobile verified at 390px with no horizontal overflow. Search index rebuilt — 14 records at 100% coverage. **One diagram fault caught by eye that every automated check passed:** Figure 1's chain was drawn as bare line segments with no arrowheads, while its own caption turns on the difference between the forward arrows and the one dashed arrow running back to the paper — direction was the entire content of the figure and was not on the page. Arrowheads added to all five, the return hop's drawn solid against its dashed arc. Figure 2's box 05 was also relabelled "THE PRESS RELEASE" → "THE PRESS RELEASE, NOT THE PAPER," since the short form dropped the contrast that names the error. **One error caught at ship time, and it is the page's own subject pointed at the page:** the essay said **sixteen** receipts in seven places — hero, table caption, *Held honestly*, meta description, `og:` and `twitter:` descriptions, JSON-LD — plus the index card, the changelog entry and this ledger row. **The table has seventeen rows.** The number was written once from the draft outline and then copied outward nine times without anyone counting the table, which is precisely mode two (a figure repeated everywhere and checked nowhere) committed by a piece whose thesis is that this happens. Counted programmatically from the rendered DOM rather than by eye, confirmed as seventeen distinct rows with no duplicate, and corrected in all ten places. Recorded rather than quietly fixed, because a page that publishes other people's uncounted numbers and hides its own would be the exact failure it names. **2026-08-11 — mode 01's worked example was corrected, and the correction is the point.** The page said the "harvesting sunlight" line was a compression Sagan never said. He said it on camera; see No. 6's row. The entry now carries both failures — the altered quote and our own over-correction — and names the shared root: *checking one medium and concluding about all of them*. This is the page's own "a debunking can be a misconception" refusal turning out to apply to the page. Caught by **Ryan Boren**. **Also 2026-08-11, an open item found while setting an epigraph on the L★S broadside:** the three-sentence Sagan quotation the house has been circulating ends "a way for the **Universe** to know itself." Every external source consulted gives "**cosmos**", and none gives "Universe" — and **this ledger already has "a way for the cosmos to know itself" VERIFIED** at No. 18, which prints it correctly. So the collection contradicts itself in public: `the-garden-and-the-stars-zine.html` is right, while `love-you-down-to-your-star-stuff.html` (5 instances, and marked *pending first check*) and `eternal-sunshine-zine.html` (1) are not, as are stimpunks.org's star-stuff page and Helen's More Realms post. **Not yet fixed anywhere** — logged in Open items. The broadside sets only the two attested sentences, in the "cosmos" wording; the widely-quoted opening "The Cosmos is within us" was **cut rather than shipped unverified**, because the book text is lending-restricted and it could not be confirmed. |
| Who Is Holding the Candle | 2026-08-15 | VERIFIED — one open item, disclosed on the page | Newly authored Foundations essay, and the companion underneath *Too Good to Check*: that one argues *go back to the primary source*, this one argues that checking is only one of two required modes and that somebody particular always has to do the going. **Every quotation was verified against page images of the primary texts supplied by Ryan Boren from his own copies** — not from a quote site, not from a search summary, and not from the Stimpunks page that first paired them. **Carl Sagan**, *The Demon-Haunted World: Science as a Candle in the Dark* (Random House, 1995), opening chapter, confirmed at Kindle loc. 89–94 of 7157: "My parents were not scientists. They knew almost nothing about science. But in introducing me simultaneously to skepticism and to wonder, they taught me the two uneasily cohabiting modes of thought that are central to the scientific method." Verbatim, including the American *skepticism*. **A second Sagan passage was added from the same page image** because it is on the facing column and materially changes the reading — he goes on to say there were *no* inspirational science teachers in his schooling, only rote memorization, "no soaring sense of wonder… and nothing about mistaken ideas that everybody had once believed." Both modes came from parents who knew no science, and neither came from the institution. Quoted with one marked elision. **Dorion Sagan**, *Cosmic Apprentice: Dispatches from the Edges of Science* (University of Minnesota Press, 2013), introduction, "Condensed — The Questing Spirit," confirmed at Kindle loc. 70–94 of 4589. Three passages: the alembic/electrum passage on science and philosophy as an antidote to each other and on technoscience losing its compass to its funders; "Science's spirit is philosophical… critical inquiry combined with fact-checking… appealing to data, not authority, which does not like to admit it is wrong"; and the passive-voice passage. **Two renderings in circulation were checked against the page image.** The alembic quotation as commonly excerpted — including in the version handed over for this page — begins "Science's eye for detail," and the page image shows the full sentence opens "I would say that applies to the flip side of the above flip takedown:". Our quotation is therefore started at the clause boundary *after* the colon, which needs no ellipsis and is standard, and the omitted words are a discourse marker pointing back at the science-versus-philosophy joke above them rather than anything load-bearing; noted here rather than on the page, where a note would be noise. The passive-voice quotation is the different case — it genuinely begins mid-sentence, and the page says so. Its elided setup ("In the counterintuitive calculus of writing genres, 'fiction' is nonfiction and nonfiction is fiction. By that I mean that") is **summarized in the prose rather than quoted**, because it is doing rhetorical work the excerpt would otherwise inherit unexplained. **OPEN ITEM, disclosed on the page in its own sources note:** the Niels Bohr line — "while the opposite of a trivial truth is false, the opposite of a great truth is another great truth" — reaches us *through* Dorion Sagan, who introduces it with "as Niels Bohr impressed." Dorion writing it is primary-confirmed; **Bohr saying it is not.** The attribution on the page is therefore "Niels Bohr, as quoted by Dorion Sagan," which is what we can stand behind; the source most often given elsewhere is a son's recollection rather than Bohr's own writing, and that has not been checked here. This is failure mode 01 caught *before* publication rather than after, and the page says so. **Donna Haraway**, "Situated Knowledges: The Science Question in Feminism and the Privilege of Partial Perspective," *Feminist Studies* 14(3), 1988 — cited for the concept and **characterized, not quoted**; page range deliberately omitted rather than reproduced unverified, which would be mode 02 on a page about mode 02. **Derivative claims** inherit their existing rows: the mycorrhizal correction (Nos. 5, 26, FG 6), Orion's Belt (No. 9), the MLK/Baldwin and distaste/disgust corrections credited to **Helen Edgar** (FG 6, No. 33), and the five-sigma argument (No. 40). **The guard is stated on the page's face:** a callout naming pseudoscience, scientism and the smoothed popular retelling, and the line "if this page ever starts reading as anti-science, it has gone wrong" — because the Dorion funding passage is the most misreadable thing quoted here. **Refusals** name relativism, using situatedness to skip the check, anti-science, insider-only speech, blanket contempt for the passive voice, and any claim that the two modes have been reconciled. |

| The Hatchery (twelve cosmic eggs) | 2026-08-14 | VERIFIED (three open items) | First check, done during the build. A **specimen sheet**, filed in *Easter Eggs*, holding the twelve drawings behind the new footer doorway; unnumbered on purpose, because the numbers are chronological and load-bearing and a sheet of drawings for arguments already published does not need a date stamped into the sequence. **Every scientific claim on it is one this site had already checked, and each entry links to the piece holding the primary citation rather than restating one from memory** — leatherback ossicles, the 1,280 m dive and the 18 °C differential, the Arrau's 2,122 sounds in eleven types with hatchlings calling from inside the egg, and painted-turtle supercooling below −10 °C all sit at **Field Guide No. 10**; monotropism at Murray, Lesser &amp; Lawson (*Autism*, 2005); the double empathy problem at Milton (*Disability &amp; Society*, 2012), worked through in No. 33. **Three of the twelve deliberately restate errors we made ourselves**, because the page is about drawings attached to claims and the failure mode is a confident illustration carrying an unchecked one: bone's piezoelectricity is **collagen's, not hydroxyapatite's** (Fukada &amp; Yasuda, 1957 — hydroxyapatite is centrosymmetric, which rules out classical piezoelectricity), **lithium, beryllium and boron break the &ldquo;made in stars&rdquo; line**, and Margulis published the 1967 endosymbiosis paper **as Lynn Sagan**, reviving Mereschkowsky and Wallin. **One new claim was researched for this page:** eggshell porosity, from S. G. Tullett, &ldquo;Pores and gas exchange of avian eggs: a review&rdquo; (*Journal of Experimental Zoology* Supplement, 1987) — pores are the only channels for molecular exchange between embryo and environment, and pore number scales with egg mass from ~300 in a 1 g egg to ~30,000 in a 500 g egg. **The world-egg traditions are named and not retold** (Hiraṇyagarbha in the *Rigveda*; the Ogdoad at Hermopolis; Ilmatar's eggs in the *Kalevala*; Pangu; Ta&#8217;aroa in the Society Islands; the Dogon Amma; Zoroastrian *Zādspram*), each stated at the level of &ldquo;this exists here,&rdquo; **none of them drawn** — the same line Field Guide No. 10 drew around its five *turtles people made*. The page also declines the popular *every culture had the same idea* smoothing, on the grounds that the resemblance is real and the explanation is not settled. **A collision with our own argument is stated on the page rather than resolved quietly:** the door mark began as a spiky profile, which **No. 21 *Not a Line*** refuses as *a better chart is still a chart* (after Patrick Dwyer, 2020, and Helen Edgar, 2026), so the drawing keeps the jag and drops the baseline and the grid, and the entry says so. **One drawing was cut in review for making a claim the physics does not support:** a *Clone Row* of twelve identical eggs originally said a single crack &ldquo;can run through every one of them,&rdquo; which is not how a monoculture fails — nothing propagates between separate individuals; the vulnerability is shared, so whatever defeats one defeats all and no resistant individual exists for selection to find. Ryan caught it. It was replaced by **The Breathing Shell** rather than patched, because the drawing itself was the wrong picture. **Open:** (1) the eggshell pore figures are a published *scaling relationship*, not a measured count for a hen's egg, and the page says so and gives no chicken-specific number; (2) Tullett is cited from its publication record and abstract rather than read in full; (3) the world-egg traditions are named from a general reference rather than from each primary text, which is the level of claim being made but is worth noting. Contrast measured on screen and print (181 elements, 0 failures) after clearing a **+bg warning of 16 elements** from a literal `rgba(15,15,42,…)` on two panels — replaced with the `--card`/`--deep` token so it inverts on paper — and a seventeenth from the ink-on-gold badge, which becomes a rule in print. Markup, sitemap and search-index gates clean. |
| The Quillery (eight Esmx) | 2026-08-15 | VERIFIED — one citation corrected pre-publication | First check, done during the build. A **specimen sheet**, filed in *Easter Eggs*, the second after The Hatchery and the first about **somebody else's character** — which is the reason it needs a licensing paragraph before it needs a science one. **Esmx the Porkypine is Kaya Oldaker's**, commissioned by Stimpunks and released **CC BY-SA 4.0** with an explicit invitation on its own page to customise and make variants; this site is already CC BY-SA 4.0, so share-alike is satisfied, and the credit line — &ldquo;Esmx&rdquo; by Kaya Oldaker is licensed under CC BY-SA 4.0 — is set in the colophon in the form the source uses. **The original artwork is not reproduced and not traced.** The figure was rebuilt from scratch in this site's line after studying the official PNGs at full size; the two ear details Kaya's own alt text singles out (an earring in one ear, a missing chunk in the other) are treated as load-bearing rather than decorative, and every drawing carries real alt text because **the source page asks people to** (&ldquo;If you share these, include alt text&rdquo;). Design facts — pig + porcupine, the rainbow mane mixing autism-spectrum and LGBT+ flag colours, the punk mohawk, the goblin-mode reference, the Sonic/Pok&eacute;mon influence, the name from **Esme / Esmeralda Weatherwax** and the Discworld witches, the *x* for gender neutrality, they/them — are all stated on the mascot's own page, which is **primary for its own account of itself** and is cited as such. **Claims checked, one per drawing.** (1) *Helium*: the 1868 solar identification — Janssen at Guntur, 18 Aug 1868; Lockyer independently, 20 Oct 1868; named for *helios*; not isolated terrestrially until **Ramsay, 1895** (Cleve and Langlet independently), a 27-year gap — per the American Physical Society's *This Month in Physics History*. (2) *Quills are modified hairs, of keratin*: standard mammalogy; the barb mechanics are **read at the primary abstract** — Cho, Ankrum, Guo, Chester, Yang, Kashyap, Campbell, Wood, Rijal, Karnik, Langer & Karp, &ldquo;Microstructured barbs on the North American porcupine quill enable easy tissue penetration and difficult removal,&rdquo; *PNAS* **109**(52), 21289&ndash;21294 (2012), doi:10.1073/pnas.1216441109, whose finding is the counter-intuitive half: the barbs raise removal force **and lower penetration force**, and the page says so rather than keeping only the tidy direction. (3) *Porcupines cannot throw quills*: the release-on-contact mechanism, after **Uldis Roze**, *Porcupines: The Animal Answer Guide* (Johns Hopkins University Press, 2012). The card is deliberately framed as the collection's standard reframe move — the popular verdict corrected about the **animal**, never about anyone's telling of it — the same line No. 47 and Field Guide No. 13 hold. (4) *Spines evolved independently*: stated across **four different mammal orders** (Rodentia, Eulipotyphla, Afrosoricida, Monotremata) rather than as the narrower and more contested Hystricidae-vs-Erethizontidae claim, which was **drafted and cut** — the cross-order version needs no hedge, and the within-porcupine version rested only on aggregator sources. (5) *Sulfur*: disulfide cross-linking as the source of hard &alpha;-keratin's stiffness is standard protein biochemistry; sulfur's origin is given as hydrostatic or explosive **oxygen burning**, in massive stars and Type Ia supernovae, after Chin, Henkel et al., &ldquo;Interstellar sulfur isotopes and stellar oxygen burning&rdquo; (arXiv:astro-ph/9505067). This is the sheet's LYDTYSS card and the only one making the whole-site claim, so it is stated as **the same atoms rather than a rhyme** — which is a stronger claim than this house usually allows itself, and is allowed here precisely because it is elemental identity and not analogy. (6) *The spiky profile*: see the open item. (7) *Constellations are lines of sight*: carried at the level No. 9 and Field Guide No. 1 state it, and the card **names No. 9's own correction** — that its depth argument originally ran on Orion's Belt, whose three stars are at essentially one distance and genuinely related — rather than repeating the superseded example. (8) *The name*: primary, as above. **Two things deliberately left out, recorded on the page so nobody adds them later:** the goblin-mode strand is not drawn, because it is the character's posture rather than a feature; and the porcupine-is-not-a-pig etymology joke was cut as a joke about a word rather than a fact about an animal. **Render checks (measured, not assumed):** 209 screen and 202 print text elements including 27 SVG labels, **zero AA failures in either medium**; 406 text boxes, 0 collisions, 0 clipped — reached after `check-overlap.mjs` caught two real faults, a 44-character label at 7.4/0.6 tracking running ~222 units wide in a 400-unit viewBox so that it was both clipped by its own SVG and overhanging far enough to land on the body paragraph beside it, plus a second clipped label on card 7; markup clean, badge and collection card added in the same pass; sitemap entry added; 13 print pages, and the figure was **checked on a print render rather than reasoned about** — Esmx's own colours are set through custom properties, so the shared sheet's attribute-matched fill inversions never touch them and Esmx prints as Esmx, with only the pale face needing a light grey so the muzzle keeps an edge on white. **Shipped with a second door on `index.html`** &mdash; Esmx at 88px beside the 104px cosmic egg, in a shared centred row, sized unequally on purpose so the egg stays the collection door and Esmx is the door to one piece; both labelled, both keyboard-reachable, both hidden in print, and the footer sits outside the search extraction root so the new label is no more indexed than the existing one (checked, not assumed: `Print freely` is not in the index either). **One citation caught before it shipped, and it is the failure mode this house documents most.** Esmx exists to evoke the **spiky profile**, so card 6 had to say whose the idea is. A draft credited it to **Doyle (2020)** — reasoning that its wording is the one most quoted, that it is what Esmx's own page cites, and that Doyle's name appeared nowhere in this repository. **That reasoning is the error, not a finding.** A paper that defines a phrase in order to work with it is *using* the term; use is not provenance, and the citation belongs to whoever the idea came from. **Caught by Ryan Boren before publication.** The spiky profile is carried as this ledger already holds it — **Damian Milton, building on Francesca Happé** (No. 8) — and Doyle (2020), *British Medical Bulletin* **135**(1), 108–125, doi:10.1093/bmb/ldaa021, read at the primary this pass, is cited only for the two phrases actually quoted from it and is **explicitly labelled a use rather than an origin** on the card, in the page footer, and in the changelog. Worth keeping in this file because the draft's mistake looked *more* rigorous than the correct answer: it swapped a community attribution for a DOI, which is the exact substitution `too-good-to-check.html` catalogues, committed while building a page about checking things |

The figure was right when written and went stale when Field Guide No. 16 shipped. Corrected, and logged publicly. |

## Open items

- **Site-wide: the *Hogfather* passages are cited to an aggregator and a screen adaptation outside this repo.** No. 49 closed this on Star Stuff by checking the novel, but the same wording — sentence case, no italics, sourced to Goodreads and to a YouTube clip of the 2006 Sky adaptation — still stands in **Ryan's commonplace book** and in the ***Becoming* glossary entry at stimpunks.org**. The novel sets Death in small capitals and italicises *become*, which is the word the whole argument rests on. A fix here does not fix it there.
- **Site-wide: the Sagan "know itself" quotation is altered in six places, and we have not fixed
  them yet.** The house circulates a three-sentence quotation ending "a way for the **Universe** to
  know itself." Every external source consulted gives "**cosmos**", and this ledger already records
  "a way for the cosmos to know itself" as **VERIFIED** at No. 18 — which prints it correctly.
  Wrong in `love-you-down-to-your-star-stuff.html` (5 instances; the page is still *pending first
  check*) and `eternal-sunshine-zine.html` (1). It has also travelled **outside this repo**, to
  stimpunks.org's star-stuff page and to Helen Edgar's More Realms post, in both text and a graphic —
  so a fix here does not fix it there, and Helen needs telling rather than being corrected silently.
  Separately, the opening sentence "The Cosmos is within us" could **not** be confirmed at all: the
  archive.org copy of *Cosmos* is lending-restricted and the Cosmos subtitle item is dark. Decide
  whether it is book, broadcast, or neither before it is set anywhere again. The L★S broadside sets
  only the two attested sentences, in the "cosmos" wording.
- **No. 6: pin the episode.** Sagan's spoken "We are star stuff. Harvesting starlight." is confirmed
  against the footage by Ryan Boren, but the episode number is not recorded. Pin it before the line
  is cited again.


- **No. 45: four papers cited from secondary reporting, none read at the primary.** The zine's two
  load-bearing sources *were* read at source (Murray 2011 and Romano, Behrendt & Burkert 2024, both
  abstracts at the publisher; Indebetouw et al. 2014 likewise), and every Helen Edgar quotation was
  read on her own pages. These four were not, and each is flagged in the zine's own colophon rather
  than only here: **Lee, Papanastassiou & Wasserburg (1976)** on the <sup>26</sup>Mg excess in
  Allende; **Tang & Dauphas (2012)** on early-solar-system <sup>60</sup>Fe/<sup>56</sup>Fe; the
  **SN 1987A dust composition-and-timing dispute** (Sarangi & Cherchneff 2015, Dwek & Arendt 2015,
  Wesson et al. 2015); and the **four standard SNR evolutionary stages**, given from general
  reference. None of them carries the argument — the contested <sup>26</sup>Al ending is presented
  as contested on purpose — but the 1976 paper in particular is a founding measurement being cited
  at second hand, which is the class of thing this ledger exists to not do quietly.
- **No. 45: Murray (2011) preprint and published paper disagree, and the zine prints the
  disagreement.** arXiv:1007.3270v1 gives GMC lifetimes of **17 ± 4 Myr** ("about two free-fall
  times") and ε<sub>ff</sub> = 0.16–0.24; the published *ApJ* 729, 133 gives **27 ± 12 Myr** ("a bit
  less than three") and ε<sub>ff</sub> = 0.14–0.24. There is only one arXiv version, so the preprint
  was never updated to match. We cite the published paper as the version of record and say so on
  spread six. **Not an open error — logged so that a future check does not "correct" the zine to the
  preprint's number**, which is the likelier direction of the mistake, since the preprint is the copy
  that is free to read.
- **No. 36: confirm the author line on Wilson & Krause (2013).** Crossref's record for
  *Aquatic Mammals* 39(1), 89–96 lists **Alexander Wilson alone**; every contemporaneous press
  account (Science, National Geographic, Oceana) names **both Wilson and Jens Krause** as the
  observers, and the zine and ledger cite it as Wilson & Krause. *Aquatic Mammals* metadata is
  thin for Short Notes, so this is most likely an indexing gap rather than an error — but it is
  an author attribution, which is exactly the class of thing this ledger exists to not guess at.
  Needs the article PDF. Until then the citation stands as-is with this note.
- **No. 36: the "entirely agranular" cetacean neocortex wants one more primary source.** The
  claim is carried by Marino et al. (2008), which states the absent-or-underdeveloped layer IV
  directly, and that is enough to ship. But the stronger blanket phrasing ("the *entire*
  cetacean neocortex is agranular") is widely attributed to Hof & Van der Gucht (2007), whose
  abstract does not contain it — the full text presumably does, and Wiley returned 403. If the
  full text confirms it, cite it there too; if it doesn't, the page is already worded to survive
  that, since it hangs the claim on the 2008 review.
- **Collection pages: get Helen Edgar's sign-off on the verbatim quote on the Star Gazing page.**
  She named the collection and described it — *"awe inspiring and not definite … possibilities of
  connecting things and ideas"* — in an informal message relayed by Ryan, and the page quotes it as
  hers in quotation marks, lightly normalized for print (hyphens → em dashes, a comma inserted at a
  line break). The attribution is certainly right; what is unconfirmed is consent to publish the
  words verbatim, which our own attribution rules ask for by name. Either she okays it, or it
  becomes a clearly-marked paraphrase — a one-line edit either way. Flagged rather than shipped
  quietly, since the alternative is exactly the pattern this ledger exists to prevent.

- **Sibling sweep, 2026-08-09 (triggered by the Manifesto's nucleosynthesis overclaim).** Six other
  pages carried the same error in milder form — "every atom heavier than **hydrogen** was forged
  inside a star," which sweeps in helium (mostly primordial) and lithium, beryllium and boron (not
  stellar). Corrected to "almost/essentially/nearly every atom heavier than **helium**… in stars and
  their deaths" on `index.html`, `about.html`, `design.html`, `elements-field-guide.html`,
  `lydtyss-zine.html` (two places) and `love-you-down-to-your-star-stuff.html` (two places). The
  phrasing also fixes a second slip in the LYDTYSS zine, which had said "forged in a **stellar
  interior**" — wrong for the r-process elements its own companion field guide credits to
  neutron-star mergers. **Note:** this was a targeted one-claim fix. It does **not** constitute a
  first check for the LYDTYSS page or the L★S Broadside; both remain pending above. `about.html`,
  `design.html` and Field Guide 2 were already `VERIFIED` and this slipped past all three — the
  claim reads as settled background, which is exactly when it stops being read.
